- **A plan awaiting approval is a waiting state.** The bridge (`modes/plan.ts` `settlePending`) emits `plan_proposal_resolved {planFilePath}` whenever a shown proposal stops waiting: approved or rejected from any window, device or agent, a turn starting while it waits (a prompt replies to it as feedback, as the card's refine does), plan mode left, a session switch. The pool holds `PoolEntry.planProposal` from `plan_proposal` to that frame (`SidecarPool.pendingPlan`; an omp exit drops it) and forwards both to hidden tabs; every window's `use-plan-approval.ts` clears the card unless its own answer is in flight (that keeps its outcome notice). `ControlTab.plan` makes the session waiting everywhere asks do (`waits()` in `service.ts`: listing `waiting for plan approval: "<title>" (<path>)`, `ompdesk_session_result` wait, no auto-close, no merge, review item `waiting`). `_answer` on it sends the card's own `plan_approval`: `approve: true` with `choice` `execute` (default) / `compact` / `keep_context` (approving follows the tool-approval rule, `approveEnabled`, implied by full control), or `approve: false` with `text` as feedback (always allowed); `save` stays the person's. Pinned by `service.test.ts` (plan cases), `sidecar-pool.test.ts`, `PlanApprovalDialog.test.tsx` and the plan tests of `e2e/agent-control.e2e.ts` (the fake omp mirrors `settlePending`).
- A message another session's agent sends arrives as a stock prompt wrapped in `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">` (`wrapAgentMessage`; a body cannot forge or close it), shown as "에이전트가 보냄 · title" (`MessageBubble`/`promptDisplayText`).
- The user can hide a session from agents (Sidebar session menu › "다른 에이전트가 이 세션을 보지 못하게", `agentHidden` in the `sidebar` prefs blob, this computer's sessions): it lists as hidden and refuses read, send, wait, answer, show and stop.
- Autopilot hooks (optional host deps `isStopping`, `beforeFirstPrompt`; `AgentControl.childrenOf`, `clearQueue`, `openSessions(caller, sessions, tag)`) keep `src/main/autopilot*.ts` out of this module.
- Tests: `agent-control/*.test.ts`, `shared/agent-control.test.ts` (default resolution: unset → manage on, approve off; stored `false`/`true` honoured), `AgentControlRow.test.tsx`, `e2e/agent-control.e2e.ts` (fake omp: create → send → wait → read, the delete card for a foreign session, an approval), `e2e/agent-app-control.e2e.ts` (no card by default, the card with the switch stored off, a deleting action asks either way), opt-in `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts`. The fake omp implements `get_last_assistant_text`, `agent_control.confirm` and `agent_control.note`.

### App control (`ompdesk_app_list`, `ompdesk_app`)
- Changes the app itself in main; arguments are validated against the action's schema. `app-catalog.ts` actions, `app-settings.ts` one `settings.<row>` action per Settings row, `app-control.ts` the one dispatch point, `app-guard.ts` and `app-channels.ts` the agent's own default-deny risk tables. `AGENT_CHANNELS` is typed over every `IpcChannel`: a new channel does not compile until it is `never` with a reason or lists the actions that call it. `IPC_POLICY` is the web device's table, not the agent's.
- `read`/`change` are free; a change is journaled in the caller's session (`agent_control_note`) and shown as a quiet toast with one-step undo when a previous value exists (`AGENT_CONTROL_APP` → `lib/agent-app.ts`, applied live).
- `sensitive` raises no card while full control is on. With it off, it raises the same `[OMPdesk]` card only while `agentControlManage` (default on) is off, **except the actions whose `manage` rule is `always`** (`ManageRule` in `app-settings.ts`, a field of `AppAction` and of a Settings row): every `sensitive` action must decide `{ lifts: reason }` or `{ always: reason, when? }`; `app-catalog.test.ts` fails on one that does not, and `app-control.test.ts` pins the `always` list (`agent_power.set`, `fullOnly`, is pinned on its own). `always` asks whatever the manage switch says because the action erases data (`sessions.delete`, `memory.purge`, `memory.delete_page`, `memory.sweep`), runs code (`extensions.install_plugin`, `extensions.install_mcp`), touches credentials or traffic (`accounts.github_sign_in`, `accounts.provider_sign_in`, `settings.proxy`), exposes the computer (`mobile_web.set_enabled` when turning it on, `remote.pair`) or ends running work (`updates.apply`, `app.quit`). The rest lift (`updates.check`, the four auto-update switches, `builtinRea`, `rotation`); an `undo` of a setting asks by its row's rule.
- `autopilot.*` actions call `ompdesk_autopilot` (its card and folder rules); `get_autopilot`/`set_autopilot` are `never` for app actions.
- `never` refuses, whatever the switches say: secrets, tokens and pairing codes, answering a card, `RPC_COMMAND_FOR_TAB` and any raw RPC passthrough. While full control is off it also refuses the agent-power switches (the control tools switch, `agentControlFull`/`agentControlManage`/`agentControlApprove`, `tools.approvalMode` and every approval, memory and computer-use switch), which `agent_power.set` reaches only under full control. It holds whether reached as a pref, a stock setting, an RPC command or one command inside another (`ActionContext` re-checks every primitive with the current full-control state; a nested RPC command is `never`).

### The `ompdesk` skill
- Kernel plus references: `SKILL.md` (at most 60 lines: tools, when to open a session, definition of done, the brief template with Ownership and Observable acceptance, handoff vs supervise, reuse a finished child, "silence is a checkpoint, not failure") links `skill://ompdesk/references/<file>.md` (`orchestration`, `worktrees-review`, `schedules`, `across`, `autopilot`, `app-control`), resolved against the skill's own `baseDir` inside an extension root (`internal-urls/skill-protocol.ts`). Each rule sits where it is used (e.g. never follow an absolute path in a child's answer into the main checkout: in the collect and compare steps). The kernel teaches the opener's loop (dispatch, end the turn, be woken, judge with `ompdesk_compare`, merge) instead of polling.
- `skill-drift.test.ts` fails when a tool or parameter in `tools.ts` and the skill disagree (renamed, added, or a word on a kernel tool line that is no parameter or enum value of it), when a `skill://` link names a missing file, or when the kernel passes 60 lines.
- There is no "remember" control tool: an agent saves durable learnings with ai-memory's own MCP tools (the skill points at `ai-memory-durable-pages`).

### Worker contract
- `#openOne` sends each child `agent_control_worker {from}` (local handler → bridge `agent_control.worker`, free in `quota-commands.ts`) once ready and before its first prompt. The child journals one `ompdesk.worker` custom entry; `resources/gui-bridge/agent-control.ts` registers the `ompdesk-worker` prompt section (`registerPromptSection`), appending `WORKER_CONTRACT` for the main agent of a session whose branch holds that entry.
- `WORKER_CONTRACT`: stay in your folder and the named scope; done = the message's acceptance checked; the final answer names changes, the check run and what is left, and ends with `Result: ok` or `Result: problem — <reason>`; report done/question/blocked with `ompdesk_report` instead of waiting to be asked.
- Journal-based, so a relaunched child keeps it; a session the user opened, the parent and a worker's own subagents never get it. Pinned by `bridge-agent-worker.test.ts` and `service.test.ts` (fan-out); the fake omp answers `agent_control.worker`.

### Worktrees and the review list
- `ompdesk_session_open` with `worktree` makes each child's checkout and branch through `worktree_create` (`omp/agent/<slug>`, base `HEAD` or `default`). Files: `branches.ts`, `stores/agent-review.ts`.
- `ompdesk_compare` reads each child's branch (`git merge-base`, `diff --numstat`, commits ahead, uncommitted and untracked counts) plus its final answer; it **runs no tests** (a child reports them).
- **Where a branch lands** (`branchFacts`/`landingFacts`, one rule): the parent checkout's HEAD and the upstream its branch tracks (`<branch>@{upstream}`, else `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`; refs as last fetched, never fetches, never touches the parent checkout). Agents push their own work (`git push origin HEAD:main`) while the parent's local main stays behind, so a tip in either has **landed** (`BranchFacts.merged`) and its diff is measured from that tip (only uncommitted work is left). An unlanded branch is measured from its merge-base with the upstream when the upstream holds the parent's HEAD, else with the parent's HEAD, so a stale local main never lends a child the upstream's newer commits (it once showed every pushed child as "200 files · +40520 −425").
- `ompdesk_merge` merges into the parent checkout's current branch with `git merge --no-ff` (local only, never a push, no remote command in the module). It refuses a detached parent, uncommitted work in child or parent, and a branch with nothing to merge (`mergeProblem`); asks one `[OMPdesk]` card only while `agentControlManage` (default on) is off; aborts a conflicting merge (`git merge --abort`) naming the files. Local sessions only.
- **The work board has no review section** (the user's decision): the agent that opened a child reviews and lands it itself with `ompdesk_compare`, `ompdesk_merge` and `ompdesk_session_stop`; nobody clicks Approve, Discard or Dismiss on a board (desktop or phone strip), and `agent_control_review` only lists. `AgentControl.reviewItems` stays: pushed as `AGENT_CONTROL_REVIEW` after a 400 ms settle on child activity and asked once on mount via the `agent_control_review` command `dispatchRpcCommand` answers itself, it feeds the "Opened sessions" rows (title, status, step, clock, report, waiting) and the opener's next-prompt children. A child that needs the person (a question, a blocker, a plan waiting for approval: `AgentReviewItem.waiting`) shows as a waiting row and a notification. No item when nothing is left: idle (no turn, no open ask), branch landed, no uncommitted or untracked file, not failed, not waiting on an answer (its opened row reads merged). A landed branch with leftovers lists only those.
  - The review list follows the world, not only tab activity: besides the pushes after a child's activity and after `ompdesk_session_open`/`_merge`/`_stop`/`_autopilot`, `AgentControlRuntime.#sweepAll` (the 10 s timer that also runs the auto-close pass; `OMP_GUI_TEST_AGENT_SWEEP_MS` in e2e) re-reads each opener's `reviewItems` and sends `AGENT_CONTROL_REVIEW` only when the list differs from the one last sent (`#reviewSent`), so a commit, push or merge made outside the app moves a child's row without tab activity: an idle child whose branch has landed (an untouched branch counts: its tip is in the parent's HEAD) and has no uncommitted or untracked file has no item. `e2e/agent-review.e2e.ts` commits into each child's checkout from outside the app and waits for the board to list all three as opened rows, with no review section or button; `host.test.ts` pins the timer push (changed → sent, unchanged → silent).
- Item: branch → base, files and +/−, uncommitted, the answer's start; `ompdesk_compare` reads the branch itself. The board shows none of it as buttons. A conflicting `ompdesk_merge` aborts the merge, names the files and keeps the child open. `ompdesk_session_stop` `close` closes the child's tab; `delete` deletes its session and, for a worktree child the caller opened on this computer, then removes its worktree and branch through `removeSafeWorktree` (`branches.ts`: a registered linked worktree only, clean incl. untracked, tip in the parent's HEAD or upstream, an untouched branch included; `worktree remove` without `--force`, `branch -d`). Anything unlanded keeps both, and the result names the path, branch and what is unlanded (`#dropWorktree`; pinned by `branches.test.ts` and `service.test.ts`).
- **Auto-close** (`AgentControl.autoClose`, `auto-close.ts` `autoCloseDecision`, run by `AgentControlRuntime` 1 s after tab activity, after a merge and every 10 s (`sweepMs`; `OMP_GUI_TEST_AGENT_SWEEP_MS` in e2e) because a merge or push outside the app moves no tab). An opened session (not the person's own, not an Autopilot child or run) closes its TAB (the session file stays; `AgentControlViewEvent.auto` → the renderer's `closeTab`, which refuses a tab on screen or with a composer draft: `lib/agent-auto-close.ts`) once it is idle with a final answer, nothing running (`isSettled`), ends with `Result: ok`, and has no branch or a landed branch with a clean worktree.
  - `parseWorkerResult` (`shared/agent-control.ts`) reads the last line only, never prose. `problem` keeps the session open as a problem row (review item `failed`, ✕); a missing line leaves it done but open.
  - Landed (`landingFacts`): tip in the parent's HEAD or upstream AND a commit of its own (an untouched branch has nothing to land); uncommitted or untracked files keep it open, reason `uncommitted`.
  - Never: a tab the person looks at (`active`/`visible`/viewer), one with a draft (`SidecarPool.hasDraft`) or a prompt of their own (`noteUserPrompt` in `dispatchRpcCommand`; agent messages are wrapped), a failed ending, unmerged commits, files left in the worktree, and not before the opener read the answer (`result`/`read`/`compare`/`merge`) unless the opener is idle.
  - The worktree goes with the close (`removeSafeWorktree`: `worktree remove` and `branch -d`, never `--force` or `-D`; re-checks landed and clean). Recorded in pref `agentClosedChildren`.
- Tests: `auto-close.test.ts`, `branches.test.ts` (real temp repos; bare remote with a stale parent main: a pushed child is landed with no diff, an unpushed one is one commit, a landed one with leftovers shows only them), `service.test.ts` (autoClose, `reviewItems`), `WorkBoardReview.test.tsx` (no review section or button on the board), `e2e/agent-review.e2e.ts` (real git: three worktrees, compare, merge two through the tool with no card by default, close a third; the board lists only opened rows), `e2e/family-opened-close.e2e.ts` (fake omp with `OMP_GUI_TEST_SESSION_FILES=1`: the plain and the merged session close and fold into "2 done", the unmerged one stays a failed row; the merged worktree and branch are removed, the other kept).
- Opt-in real omp (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`): the third test of `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts` opens a worktree child that commits a file, compares, merges (no card by default; the test approves one if it shows), creates and lists a schedule. Stock presents host tools as `xd://` devices, so the journal shows `write` calls to `xd://ompdesk_*`.

### Beyond the caller's window
- `AgentControlHost.tabs` returns every open tab of the app, the caller's window first (`ControlTab.here`, `device`, `sessionPath`; `SidecarPool.tabWindows`/`windowOfTab`); every call to a tab goes through the window that owns it, so send, wait, answer, show and stop reach other windows and a paired device's tabs (its host's grants and `PeerQuota` apply).
- `ompdesk_sessions` takes `scope` `window` (default) | `app` | `stored`, `device` (a paired host by name or id, `local`), `folder`, `status` (`running` | `waiting` | `idle`), `mine`, `query`, `limit`, and always ends with the paired devices and their state (`AgentControlHostDeps.remote.hosts`).
- `stored` lists the session index of this computer (`SessionIndex.list`/`searchContent`) or a paired host (`sessions.list`/`sessions.search`). Rows are cached by stock id (`#stored`), so `_session_read`, `reopen` and `ompdesk_compare` take a saved id (an open saved session goes through its tab). A saved transcript is read through stock `loadSessionMessagesReadOnly` (no writer, no lock: `sessions.messages`, `StockSessionStore.messages`, `SessionIndex.readMessages`) or the host's `sessions.messages` (`LocalHostServices`; refuses a path the host's user hid, dep `agentHidden`, code `session_hidden`), paged from the end with an index cursor.
- `ompdesk_session_open` takes `device` (spawned through `spawnTab`; folder absolute or that device's workspace; no worktree off the caller's machine; an offline device refuses) and `reopen` (a saved session's file on the device holding it; never with folder/worktree/mode, never marked a worker, refused when a tab holds it).
- `ompdesk_compare` reads per session the final answer, the check-like commands its transcript ran with outcome (`checksRun`) and a worktree's branch facts. With `criteria` (non-empty strings; no count limit) main builds one judge prompt (`JUDGE_EVIDENCE_CHARS` 60 000 characters of evidence, split per session) and sends `agent_control_judge` to the CALLER's omp (local handler → bridge `agent_control.judge` → `completeOnSmol` with `role: "slow"`, purpose `ompdesk-judge`, a model command in `quota-commands.ts`). `parseVerdict` (zod) yields a ranked verdict, `yes`/`partly`/`no`/`unknown` per criterion and a winner; a reply that is not the JSON is shown as is.
- A saved row or untitled tab reads as its task (`storedTitle`, `agentTaskTitle`) or `(untitled, in <folder>)`, never the raw agent-message wrapper; an opened tab's `ControlTab.title` falls back to `IpcTabInfo.task`.
- Tests: `service.test.ts` (scopes, filters, stored list/read/paging, device open, reopen, judge, `checksRun`, `parseVerdict`), `host.test.ts` (another window, device spawn rules, stored rows local and remote), `src/main/remote/host-services.test.ts` (`sessions.messages`, hidden refused), `e2e/agent-control-across.e2e.ts` (another window, a judged compare, a closed session found/read/reopened; two paired apps: a session opened on the device, its prompt in the host's record, its transcript read back), opt-in fourth test of `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts`.

### Mailbox (`ompdesk_report`)
- A session another session opened reports with `ompdesk_report` (`kind` progress | done | blocked | question | review | note, `text`, `files`, `checks`, `to` a sibling, `dispatch`, `id`). `mail.ts` holds the rules (`Mailbox`, `deliveryFor`, `mailText`); `service.ts` `#report` routes: the opener by `AgentControlHost.parentOf` (this launch's `#parents`, else `IpcTabInfo.openedBy`, else the tab holding `openedBySession`); a sibling only under the same opener, written as `note`; nobody else. Decision gates go to the parent and the person and are never auto-resolved.
- Delivery is ONE stock custom message in the receiver (`agent_control_mail` → bridge `agent_control.mail` → `pi.sendMessage`, customType `ompdesk.mail` from `resources/gui-bridge/agent-control-contracts.ts`): question/blocked `aside` + `triggerTurn` (an idle receiver starts a turn); done/review `followUp` + `triggerTurn`; progress/note no turn (shown at once when idle, `nextTurn` while busy). Progress from one sender is coalesced for `MAIL_COALESCE_MS` (2 s, latest wins, `merged` counts the rest); any other report supersedes pending progress.
- The model reads `<ompdesk-mail from=… kind=… dispatch=…>` plus a one-line instruction (answer a question with `ompdesk_session_send`; a user-only choice goes to the user, never guessed). The transcript draws `MailNote` (`CustomMessageCard.tsx`, `⑂ Session report · <title>`, question/blocked in the warning tint, files and checks in the tooltip, `data-agent-mail`); the work board's opened rows show the last report (`AgentReviewItem.report`; `waiting` puts a child with an unanswered question first; `opened-children.ts`).
- Dispatch ids: every message an opener sends its child carries `[ompdesk dispatch dN: …]` (and `[ompdesk request <id>]` for a `request_id`); a new dispatch marks the child's question answered, and a report naming an older dispatch is ignored (`stale`).
- The journals are the record (no OMPdesk store). A report's id defaults to a hash of both session files, kind, dispatch and text, so a call stock re-runs after a relaunch has the same id; the receiver's bridge drops an id its branch already holds (`hasMailEntry` over stock's `custom_message` entries) or this process already queued. A report counts as delivered (`#seen`) only after delivery succeeded; a failed one is retried by the sender with the same id (progress `MAIL_PROGRESS_RETRIES` = 3). After a relaunch main reads dispatch and request ids back from the child's transcript (`journalMarks`), so fencing and `request_id` idempotency of `ompdesk_session_send` hold. Concurrent sends with one `request_id` (keyed by both session files) join one in-flight delivery.
- Receipts: a send answers `accepted` plus `turn started` when the target is seen running within `TURN_START_WAIT_MS` (1.5 s). No count or rate cap; only a self-send is refused.
- Tests: `service.test.ts` (delivery per kind, question waiting, stale dispatch, repeated id, coalescing, busy receiver, siblings, receipts, retried failure, fencing and request ids after a relaunch), `bridge-agent-mail.test.ts` (stock `sendMessage`, journal and process dedupe), `CustomMessageCard.test.tsx`, `opened-children.test.ts`, `e2e/agent-mail.e2e.ts` (two children report and finish, one asks and is answered, the parent's turns start on done and question with no polling tool; a report repeated after a relaunch arrives once).

### Schedules and Autopilot
- `ompdesk_schedule` `create`/`list`/`cancel` (`service.ts`; dep `schedules` of `AgentControlRuntime` ← `IpcDeps.schedules` ← `scheduleService` in `index.ts`) goes through `ScheduleService` (`state()`, `command({action:"save"|"delete"})`), so the Schedules page, its log and its one-run-per-schedule rule apply.
  - A create raises one `[OMPdesk]` card only while `agentControlManage` (default on) is off; names the schedule `[agent] <name>` (`AGENT_SCHEDULE_PREFIX`; main-owned provenance shown in Settings › Schedules; only such schedules can be cancelled by an agent); never sets `autoApprove` (a print-mode run cannot ask, so tools needing approval are denied); leaves the interval to the service's own minimum; refuses a relative folder and a paired-host caller (runs start on this computer).
  - `SCHEDULE_RUNS_KEPT` 200 (`shared/schedules.ts`) bounds the service's run log, and a start while the previous run goes is recorded as skipped; neither limits how many schedules an agent creates.
  - Tests: `service.test.ts`, `e2e/agent-schedule.e2e.ts` (the real `schedules.json`, list, cancel).
- `ompdesk_autopilot` (`action` start | status | stop; `AutopilotSource` in `service.ts`, adapted by `host.ts` over `Autopilot.handleCommand` through the optional dep `autopilot`, wired in `ipc.ts`; skill reference `references/autopilot.md`): a start goes through the one `#start` path from a local tab of the caller's window that has the folder open (default the caller's own; a paired-host caller is refused). `ship` commit/push/release maps to the sheet presets (omitted: the level of the stored `autopilotShip`, else commit; `plan.defaultShip` in `host.ts`, so an agent's start always names a level and never gets Automatic), `branch`/`advanced` 1:1 onto `AutopilotStartOptions`. It raises one `[OMPdesk]` card only while `agentControlManage` (default on) is off, and always for ship release and for the default branch, `main` or `master`, whatever the switch says (a push to main is never loosened; push and release from Autopilot keep the card the ship level needs). No cap on agent-started runs. `stop` = End, `abort` = Stop now (pauses).

### New worktree preparation
- `src/main/local/git/worktree-seed.ts`, run by `createWorktree` after `worktree add`, so the worktree dialog, Autopilot and `ompdesk_session_open` worktrees all get it (stock omp 18.12.1 has no `worktreeinclude` and no worktree hook).
- `.worktreeinclude` at the main checkout's root lists paths or globs (`*`, `?`, `**`; `#` comments) copied from the main checkout only when `git check-ignore` reports them ignored (a tracked file never is). Refused: absolute paths, `..`, `.git`, `!`, any match whose real parent leaves the checkout. A symlink inside a copied folder is recreated only when it points inside; **on Windows a file link is copied (a file symlink there needs Developer Mode or admin, and OMPdesk never requires one) and a folder link becomes a junction** (`worktree-seed.test.ts` "copies an ignored file link on Windows"; tests that make a file symlink use `itWithFileSymlinks` from `src/main/test-symlinks.ts`, skipped where the OS refuses). Budget `SEED_MAX_BYTES` 50 MB / `SEED_MAX_FILES` 10,000; existing files never overwritten; every skip reported (`WorktreeSeedReport.skipped`).
- Then `.ompdesk/worktree-setup` (`.cmd` on Windows), only when it exists in the new worktree or the main checkout, runs in the worktree under the setup supervisor (`/bin/sh <file>`, env `OMPDESK_ROOT_PATH`/`OMPDESK_WORKTREE_PATH`, `SETUP_TIMEOUT_MS` 120 s, process tree killed at the timeout and its exit code then ignored, output in `<worktree git dir>/ompdesk-worktree-setup.log`). A failure is reported in `RpcWorktreeCreateResult.setup`, never fatal.
- The dialog toasts both (`announcePreparation`, `worktree.seed*`/`worktree.setup*`); `ompdesk_session_open` adds `describePreparation` to its notes. `worktree_create`'s budget includes it (`WORKTREE_PREPARE_BUDGET_MS`; renderer, agent-control and Autopilot wait 960 s). Pinned by `worktree-seed.test.ts` (real temp repos).

### Worktree names
- Files: `shared/project-origin.ts` (rules), `main/local/git/origin.ts` (git reads), `renderer/lib/project-origin.ts` (`useProjectNames`), `stores/project-origins.ts`, channel `project-origin:get` = `window.omp.projectOrigins.get(cwds)`. A linked worktree's folder (`~/.omp/wt/gui-<name>-<hash7>`) never shows as a project name.
- Git alone says what it is: `--git-common-dir` names the main checkout (`<main>/.git`, else the first `git worktree list` entry for a bare or relocated dir); `--git-dir` ≠ common dir marks a linked worktree; the branch gives its kind (`autopilot/…` → "오토파일럿"/Autopilot, `omp/agent/…` → "병렬 작업"/Parallel work, or the child's title where a one-line `project · title` is shown; any other branch shows as itself).
- No new store: main caches each cwd for 30 s (5 s when git cannot place it); the renderer asks once per cwd and retries an unknown one after 10 s. A folder git cannot place (removed worktree, not a repository, a paired host's path) shows its own name with the `gui-…-<hash7>` dressing stripped.
- Surfaces: sidebar group right under its project's (indented, branch icon, `data-worktree-group`), labelled `Autopilot`/`Parallel work` (`acme-web · Autopilot` when the project's group is not listed), full path and project as tooltip; title bar and pane header breadcrumb `acme-web › Autopilot › <title>` (`data-titlebar-worktree`); work board head `acme-web · Autopilot` (`data-board-project`); window title `<name> — acme-web · Autopilot`; an untitled worktree tab chip reads from `IpcTabWorktree` (`baseCwd` + branch, no git); session and workspace pickers. **A new surface that shows a cwd's folder name goes through `useProjectNames`, never `basename`.**
- An Autopilot run's children (`autopilot/<run>-c<N>-<area>`, `autopilotRunOf`) have no group of their own: their rows sit in the run's group under the run's row, one step in (`data-session-run-child`; `runParentOf` in `Sidebar.tsx`, from the branch git reports or, while git cannot place a new child worktree, the child tab's `worktree.baseCwd`).
- A session another agent opened (`ompdesk_session_open`, an Autopilot child) reads as its task until stock names it: `agentTaskTitle` (`shared/agent-control.ts`: the body's `Title:` line by `assignmentTitle`, else its first line) of the indexed first message in `sessionDisplayTitle`, and before the index lists it the tab's `task` (`IpcTabInfo.task`, set via `SidecarPool.setTabTask` right after the spawn), never "New session".
- Tests: `project-origin.test.ts`, `origin.test.ts` (real temp repos: main, linked, detached, bare, removed), `Sidebar.test.tsx` "worktree groups", and the no-`gui-2026`/`gui-agent` assertions in `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts` and `e2e/agent-review.e2e.ts`.

## Panels, sidebar and work views

### History, stats, compaction limits

- Composer history uses stock `HistoryStorage` through the guarded bridge methods `history_recent`, `history_search`, `history_record`. Local tabs share history with this machine's terminal omp; remote tabs use the paired host's history and never import client prefs. Legacy `inputHistory` prefs migrate oldest-first on the first successful local bridge load, then are deleted (`e2e/history-migration.e2e.ts`). Stock `shouldSkipHistory` keeps secrets out of SQLite. Transient recall and Up/Down draft restore stay renderer-local.
- Compaction-limit commit/prefill uses stock `config/compaction-threshold` helpers through `compaction_limits`; typing never waits for bridge validation.
- Stats: `resources/gui-bridge/stats-host.ts` runs the user's omp-stats server (stock `omp stats` always opens a browser) and adds the paginated `/api/stats/requests` route stock lacks.

### Side panel

- `components/layout/PanelContainer.tsx`: header = view title, the four primary view icons (Terminal, Changes, Preview, Files), ⋮ View options (Background tasks, Plan, Logs, Queue, Tree, Memory; Autopilot only for an Autopilot run's tab), close. A view's own actions sit in its toolbar row, never in the header.
- Terminal reuses stock `@oh-my-pi/pi-natives` `PtySession` inside the stock helper's terminals worker (`resources/gui-bridge/stock-helper/terminals.ts`, `src/main/terminal/`): no second native addon, one `TerminalService` per worker (overlapping first requests share its creation), stdin EOF kills every PTY. A paired host's tab gets the host's terminals (`terminal.request`/`terminal.shells` in `remote/host-services.ts`, disposed with the host tab).
- The agent reads a tab's terminals through the read-only `gui_terminal_read` host tool (stock `set_host_tools` on every ready sidecar, answered in `ipc.ts`, `src/main/terminal/agent-tool.ts`).
- Preview: a main-managed `WebContentsView` (`src/main/preview/`) on a non-persistent partition with sandbox/contextIsolation, no preload, http(s) only; permissions, downloads and new windows refused; remote-tab loopback URLs blocked. Background tasks read stock `inspectAsyncJob`/`cancelAsyncJob` through the bridge.
- Changes polling (`panels/RepositoryChanges.tsx`, `get_git_fingerprint` in `local/git/changes.ts`): an open list asks git every 3 s only for a fingerprint (one `status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z --untracked-files=all`, whose `branch.oid` is the HEAD oid, plus an in-process `lstat` of each listed path so a second edit of a modified file moves it). The panel passes the worktree `root` its last full read reported, so `rev-parse --show-toplevel` runs only before the first read or when that root does not hold the tab's folder. 1 git process per unchanged tick vs 9 for a full `get_git_changes` (counted by trace2 in `changes.test.ts`). Only a changed fingerprint reloads the list; the open diff stays until its replacement arrives; a hidden window clears the timer; a failed reload keeps its fingerprint as baseline and waits for the checkout to change. Known gap: a stash drop or any operation with no status change does not move the fingerprint (the refresh button reads it).
- Every `git`/`gh` child gets `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` (`execBin`), so a status refresh never takes `.git/index.lock` from the agent's own git. On macOS GUI git calls run the developer dir's git directly (`gitBin` in `exec.ts`), never xcrun's `/usr/bin/git` trampoline (~3.4 ms CPU per call).
- Unsaved Files edits belong to their tab: `closeTab` on the tab named by `fileEditTabId` while `fileEditDirty` does not close it but raises the editor's discard confirm (`holdTabClose` → `pendingFileSwitch`). "Discard" sets `discardedForTabClose` and the side panel then closes the tab; Cancel keeps both.
- Subagent view (`panels/SubagentPanel.tsx`): a sidebar subagent row dragged onto a session pane (`omp:subagent-drag`, taken by `SplitWorkspace`) or its menu's "Open in side panel" sets `useUiStore.panelTab = "subagent"` + `panelSubagent {tabId, agentId}`; the view reads the transcript and steers through the owning tab's runtime. It is not in `PANEL_TABS` (default-view setting, `panel.*` shortcuts); panes stay main-owned omp tabs.
  - The transcript (`SubagentTranscript.tsx`, shared with `SubagentTranscriptSheet`) is the main transcript's own components, never a second renderer: `buildHistoryRows`/`reuseTranscriptRows` drawn by `MessageBubble`, `ProcessGroup` (compact work card, or per-message tool cards by `transcriptDetail`) and `ReadGroupCard` under `.omp-transcript-editorial.omp-subagent-conversation` (`styles/subagent-view.css`; insets follow the panel width by container query; code and tables scroll inside their block).
  - It runs in a derived `SessionRuntime` of the owning tab with private tools/messages/session stores (`hydrateMessages`, `turnIsLive` while the agent runs), so the owner's streams and goal never reach the cards. `use-subagent-transcript.ts` reads by byte offset and follows a running agent (progress frame or 4 s poll; a page with nothing new keeps the same array); `use-stick-to-bottom.ts` is ChatStream's gesture-vetoed follow plus "Jump to latest". Header is one line (provider mark + agent, status, tokens, clock, open full / stop / copy); the steer box reuses `.omp-composer-box` and `.omp-composer-action`.
  - Tests: `SubagentPanel.test.tsx`, `SidebarSubagents.test.tsx`; `e2e/subagent-view.e2e.ts` (`fixture:subagents:transcript`/`:append`, light/dark × en/ko × 380/520 px, 320 px overflow; `OMP_GUI_SUBAGENT_SHOTS=<dir>` writes captures).

### Memory (ai-memory)

- Memory is ai-memory: `src/main/ai-memory/`, contract `src/shared/ai-memory.ts`, pref `aiMemory` default **on** (only a stored `false` turns it off, `aiMemoryEnabled`). Settings › Memory (`settings/memory/MemorySection.tsx`) is the ONLY memory setting. The name in every UI string is just "Memory"/"메모리", never "ai-memory"; no link to the project's repo or docs, no credits in the UI. The licence notice lives in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` and `LICENSES/` only; Settings › Updates & about › This computer lists it as one of OMPdesk's own components (`settings/memory/MemoryComponentRow.tsx`: version, status, "updated with OMPdesk", no update control).
- Stock's `MemoryBackend` (`memory.backend` rows, the `memory` model role row via `HIDDEN_ROLE_IDS`, the bridge's `get_memory_report`/`memory_*` methods) is gone from OMPdesk. Stock keeps `memory.backend` `off` for RPC hosts itself (`protocolDefault: ["rpc", "acp"]`), so nothing is held as a runtime default.
- **Side panel Memory view** (`panels/AiMemoryPanel.tsx`, `stores/ai-memory.ts`, `/memory`; the view id stays `memory`, so saved default-view prefs keep working): a glance, not a second browser. Status and counts, **Open Memory** (Settings › Memory), "Open web console" (the local server's page), the recent pages (a row calls `useMemoryPageStore.openPage` → Settings › Memory › Pages) and the open handoffs (a row opens Handoffs). Palette command `cmd.memory` ("Memory") opens the page too.
- **Built in, not downloaded** ([ai-memory](https://github.com/akitaonrails/ai-memory), MIT): `resources/omp-builtin/ai-memory/ai-memory.json` is the single version source (version, per-platform archive and binary SHA-256).
  - `bun run sync:ai-memory` (CI and release run it before packaging) downloads the pinned macOS arm64 and Windows x64 assets, checks the published `.sha256`, the pin and the extracted binary, and writes the gitignored `resources/ai-memory-bin/<platform>-<arch>/`, which each builder config ships as `<resources>/ai-memory-bin/` outside the asar. `scripts/after-pack.cjs` `checkAiMemory` and `packaging-config.test.ts` fail a package without the binary or with another hash; `scripts/mac-sign.cjs` signs the nested binary with the app's certificate; the release's `Verify bundle` step verifies and runs it.
  - `bun run bump:ai-memory [version|latest]` moves the pin and syncs; then run `bun run gen:licenses` and `bun scripts/gen-ai-memory-notices.ts` (`LICENSES/ai-memory-LICENSE`, `LICENSES/ai-memory-dependencies.txt`); `licenses:check` fails when they lag the pin. The bundled copy updates only with OMPdesk (no CLI-tools updater entry).

#### The Memory page

- Settings › Memory (`settings/memory/`; Settings sub-target `memory:<tab>`, the tab is the route). `MemorySection.tsx` is the shell: tabs Overview, Search, Pages, Handoffs, Sessions, Profile, Maintenance, each one component; project selector `resolveProject` = the user's pick, else the project whose name ends with the active session's folder name, else the latest. Memory off/starting/failed shows only Overview.
  - **Overview**: switch, status, project, its counts, last capture (7-day activity and health in the tooltip), disk size, data folder, "Open web console" (`웹 화면 열기`, only while running) and its password, terminal omp, one privacy line (full text as tooltip).
  - **Search**: full text in the project or all projects (toggle); a hit shows title, path, kind, tier, age, snippet and opens in Pages (hits carry no tier or time on the wire; main fills them from the projects' page lists).
  - **Pages**: by kind (rules, decisions, gotchas, procedures, sessions folded, notes), markdown reader, Edit (a new version through the server's write path, keeping kind, tier, tags, pin), Pin, Helpful/Not helpful, Delete (confirm) and a **Deleted** group whose Restore reads the page from the checkpoint the delete returned (`AiMemoryDeleted.rev`, in `useMemoryPageStore.deleted` and the snapshot cache). Session pages are read-only.
  - **Handoffs**: open first; the detail reads without claiming; Cancel (confirm) discards an open one. Korean says "인계". **Sessions**: recent captured sessions with observation counts; one opens its raw observations read-only, 50 a page. **Profile**: the cross-project profile, read-only; Edit opens the entry's page in Pages. **Maintenance**: rule-based lint (writes no report), retention sweep (Preview is a dry run; Run sweep enabled only after a preview that found something, then confirms), Delete all memory (typed-word confirmation).
- UI rules: lists/detail are `MasterDetail` (detail on its own screen on a phone). Rows use `SettingsRow` `tipOnly` (every description is a tooltip, never inline; `data-setting-desc` must not appear). Row actions are quiet secondary buttons (danger only in the confirm dialog). Close rows declare `--hit-gap-x/-y` (tab bar 4 px, reader's action row 4/16 px) so no touch reach covers a neighbour.
- Data: reads go through `useMemoryRead(key, read)` (`use-memory-read.ts`: last known value at the first frame via `lib/last-read.ts`, re-read in place and when `useMemoryPageStore.version` bumps; never a spinner); every write through `useMemoryAction` (`memory-ui.tsx`: toast, then `bump()`). Strings: `locales/memory/<section>.ts` (en and ko side by side, aggregated by `locales/memory/index.ts`).
- Tests: one `*.test.tsx` per tab plus shell/component row/panel on `memory-test-harness.tsx` (fake `window.omp.aiMemory`); `e2e/memory-page.e2e.ts` on the stateful fake server (palette → Overview → search → open → edit/save → delete → restore → read/cancel a handoff → session paging → side panel), rig shared with `ai-memory.e2e.ts` in `e2e/fixture/ai-memory-rig.ts`. The responsive audit covers every tab and list → detail state (`settings-memory-<tab>`, `settings-memory-<tab>-detail` in `e2e/responsive-surfaces.ts`, answered by `dev-harness/fixture-memory.ts`); run at 390 with `OMP_GUI_RESPONSIVE_ONLY=settings-memory`.

#### Client, binary, lifecycle

- **Client** (`src/main/ai-memory/client.ts`, one `AiMemoryClient`; the token is read in main, never in the renderer): reads are `/api/v1` REST routes plus `/admin/status`, `/admin/checkpoints`, `/admin/profile/*`. Writes go through the server's own write path, never its files: `/admin/write-page`, `/admin/delete-page` (returns `pre_checkpoint`; the client falls back to the head checkpoint read before the call), `/admin/restore-page`, `/admin/lint` (always `dry_run` + `no_llm`), `/admin/forget-sweep`; `memory_feedback` and `memory_handoff_cancel` (no REST route) go as MCP `tools/call` on `/mcp` (stateless JSON, `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`).
  - Twelve IPC channels (`AI_MEMORY_OVERVIEW|PAGES|SESSIONS|OBSERVATIONS|PROFILE|LINT|SWEEP|WRITE_PAGE|DELETE_PAGE|RESTORE_PAGE|FEEDBACK|CANCEL_HANDOFF`): reads are web-`workspace`, writes `host-admin` in `IPC_POLICY`. For the agent (`app-channels.ts`) `memory.delete_page` and `memory.sweep` are `sensitive` catalog actions beside `memory.purge`; everything else is `never` (the agent has its own memory tools). `ipc.ts` validates every argument (a page path is never absolute or `..`).
- **Which binary** (`binary.ts`): the shipped file is never run in place (its path lands in a launchd unit, a Windows logon entry and omp's MCP entry, and a Windows update replaces the install folder under a running server). `stageBinary` verifies it and copies it once to `<tools>/ai-memory/<version>/` (atomic rename; old versions pruned after wiring).
  - Accepted when its bytes are the pin's `binarySha256` (Windows ships it unsigned; a dev build's synced copy) or, on macOS only, when its signature satisfies `AI_MEMORY_REQUIREMENT` = `identifier "ai-memory" and certificate root = H"<scripts/mac-signing.json certificateSha1>"` (`codesign --verify --strict -R`, `codeRequirementProblem`). Packaging re-signs the nested binary with OMPdesk's certificate (`mac-sign.cjs`) after `after-pack.cjs` checked the pin, so a release's bytes never equal the pin and any other valid signature (system binary, ad-hoc re-signed copy) fails. A package signed ad-hoc therefore has no memory, by design. `release.yml`'s `Verify bundle` checks the same requirement.
  - The staged copy is trusted only by bytes: reused when it is the pin's or byte-identical to the shipped file verified at that launch; otherwise replaced. (0.38.0/0.38.1 compared the signed bytes with the pin, failed every launch silently, so memory never started on a Mac; pinned by `binary.test.ts`, `service.test.ts`, `e2e/packaged-ai-memory.e2e.ts`.)
  - A new app version stages a new directory, so ai-memory's `setup` sees a changed unit and restarts the service on the new binary. A user's own ai-memory (PATH, `~/.local/bin`, Homebrew, the Windows script's folders) is used only when NEWER than the shipped one (data schema migrates forward only).
- **Runtime log**: `AiMemoryService` writes `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` lines with source `ai-memory`: `launch` {enabled, automatic, platform, arch, bundledDir, override} once per start, `binary` {source, version, bin}, `phase` {phase, step, detail, from, fromStep} on every change, `failed` {step, detail, stack} per failure (polls too). `start()` never rejects: whatever it throws becomes the `failed` phase, shown in Settings with its reason and Try again.
- **Lifecycle** (`service.ts`, `AiMemoryService`; started after the first tab). Only a packaged app or `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_BIN=<binary>` changes the machine; a dev build only reports. `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_NO_SERVICE=1` passes `--no-service` for tests; `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_UNIT_PIDFILE=<file>` stands in for the OS service in the fake-binary e2e.
  - The server is **ai-memory's own per-user service** (launchd LaunchAgent / Windows logon entry, installed by `ai-memory setup`), not an OMPdesk child. OMPdesk owns only its own wiring and **never writes ambient configuration**: nothing goes to `~/.omp/agent`, no other agent's config is touched, `uninstall --apply` is never run (it removes every agent's wiring).
  - On: `setup --plan --json --agent omp`; on a fresh data dir `init` plus the one config change below; then `setup --yes --agent omp` with `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` at a scratch folder (`<userData>/ai-memory/omp-ext.setup`, deleted afterwards): `setup` cannot install the service without wiring an agent, so its omp wiring lands in the scratch folder, and the shared skills it writes to `~/.agents/skills` are taken back when this run created them (`terminal.ts removeNewSharedSkills`). A lone `problem: self-check` is not fatal; the `/healthz` poll decides.
  - Every `setup --yes` runs **isolated** (`SpawnSpec.isolated`, `setup/supervisor.ts isolatedLaunch`), still under the setup supervisor. WHY (v0.38.0): the service host outlives OMPdesk by design and on Windows a child inherits every inheritable handle of its parent, so a plain spawn gave the host the app's pipes and those of whatever launched the app, open as long as the service runs (the packaged first-launch e2e's teardown timed out). Isolated, Bun starts the command (`bun -e` with `bun:ffi` calling kernel32 `CreateProcessW`, no PowerShell) with an explicit handle list (`PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST`: NUL stdin, a temp log file as stdout/stderr, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`); the supervisor passes the log on once the command exited. On macOS launchd starts the server, so it inherits nothing.
  - Bun may still be installing on a fresh Windows machine, so the wiring depends on it (dep `bunPresent`, `findBun`): without Bun a pass runs no command and fails with "Waiting for Bun…"; the setup state reporting Bun present (`index.ts` calls `AiMemoryService.bunInstalled()`) re-runs the pass once, so memory sets itself up on first launch with no relaunch or Try again.
  - Then ai-memory's OWN generators fill the **app-owned extension root** `<userData>/ai-memory/omp-ext/` (`root.ts`): `install-hooks --agent omp --apply --config-file <root>/index.ts`, `install-mcp --client omp --apply --config-file <root>/.mcp.json`, `install-skills --target-dir <root>/skills`, produced next to the root and swapped in only when bytes differ (nothing hand-written, no `.bak`, nothing touched on an unchanged launch). omp reads a package root's `index.ts`, `.mcp.json`, `skills/` (stock `omp-extension-roots.ts`), like `resources/omp-builtin/rea`.
  - Service ownership (`unit.ts`: launchd plist `com.github.akitaonrails.ai-memory` / HKCU `Run\ai-memory` runs a binary under `<tools>/ai-memory` or the binary in use) is stored as pref `aiMemoryServiceOwned`. A service OMPdesk did not start (someone else's unit, or a server already answering) gets `setup --no-service` and is never stopped.
  - Off: the pref is stored first (`extensionArgs()` returns `[]` at once), the root is deleted, and the server stops only when OMPdesk started it and no other client uses it. `uninstall` WITHOUT `--apply` is a read-only listing; any line that is not a managed skill means another agent (or the terminal omp) is wired; an unreadable listing counts as in use. Stop = `launchctl bootout` + delete the plist (macOS), delete the `Run` value + `taskkill` of the PIDs in `<data>/service-host.json` (Windows; never by image name: every live omp has an `ai-memory mcp-bridge`). The data stays.
  - Delete all memory: the same stop (owned units only), then `wiki/ db/ raw/` are deleted after `/healthz` stops answering (`uninstall --purge-data` refuses while any `ai-memory` process runs), then on again (memory off with other clients using the server: `setup` brings it back).
  - A 60 s poll (`/healthz`, `/admin/status` for counts, the root's files) heals by re-running setup and the generators (twice down in a row, or the root gone; at most once per 10 minutes).
  - Defaults stay ai-memory's zero-LLM path (no provider, no dream) and its single-user cross-project profile. The one change: a **fresh** config gets `embedding_provider = "none"` (`config.ts`), since local embeddings fetch an 87 MB model from huggingface.co and hold ~355 MB resident; search stays full-text, entity and graph, and removing that line turns hybrid search on. An existing config is never touched.
- **Coverage**: every omp OMPdesk spawns gets `-e <root>` while memory is on and the root is complete (`AiMemoryService.extensionArgs()`, read at each spawn: `sidecar-factory.ts` `aiMemoryExtensionArgs` for local tabs and tabs a host runs for a paired device, `schedules.ts` `ompRunLauncher` for scheduled `omp -p` runs; subagents are children of those omps). The terminal `omp` CLI is separate unless the user asks. Short-lived internal omps that pass `--no-extensions` (rename, model setup) capture nothing, on purpose. The brief is the folder's open handoff, injected as a hidden `ai-memory-handoff` custom message at a session's first prompt. A paired host's own OMPdesk wires its own.
- **Terminal omp** (Settings › Memory › "Also use in the terminal omp", `AiMemoryService.setTerminal`, pref `aiMemoryTerminal`; needs memory on): runs ai-memory's `setup --yes --agent omp` against omp's real agent dir (MCP entry `mcp.json`, extension `extensions/ai-memory-omp.ts`; skills to the shared `~/.agents/skills`); each later launch re-runs it so the entry follows the new staged binary. ai-memory has no per-agent uninstall, so the reverse (`terminal.ts removeTerminalWiring`) removes exactly what `setup` added: the extension only when it carries ai-memory's generated header, the `mcpServers["ai-memory"]` entry only when it runs an `ai-memory` binary or the `/mcp` URL (other servers and keys stay; the file goes only when nothing is left). Shared skills stay (other agents read them). While omp's agent dir holds the generated extension, `extensionArgs()` leaves the root out so a session never captures twice.
- **Migration** (once, in the first `enable` pass with pref `aiMemoryTerminal` unset): an earlier build wrote ai-memory's extension and MCP entry into `~/.omp/agent` with the staged binary. When the `ai-memory` MCP entry runs a binary under `<tools>/ai-memory` or the binary in use (`legacyOwned`), exactly those two entries are removed and the pref is stored `false`; wiring that runs someone else's ai-memory stays (it counts as the terminal's). Shared skills the old `setup` left in `~/.agents/skills` stay (indistinguishable from another agent's).
- **Memory quality** (`src/shared/memory-prompt.ts`, `src/main/ai-memory/{retitle,distill,distill-model,upkeep}.ts`, started by `index.ts` when the server answers):
  - **Titles**: a session page used to be titled by its first prompt's first line, so an agent-opened session read `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">` and a subagent "Complete assignment thoroughly:". `promptTitle` (import-free: the ai-memory build embeds this very file into the omp extension it generates, `native/memory` `include_str!`) strips the wrapper and stock's prefix and takes the `Title:` line (same rule as `assignmentTitle`), else the omp session title (first prompt only, never a junk one), else the first meaningful line. The extension sends it as `promptTitle` on `user-prompt`; the server titles the observation by it.
  - **Children never create or consume a handoff**: a session is a child when omp says `ctx.agent.kind === "sub"` or every prompt it received was agent-written (`nextAgentDriven`). The extension adds `subagentType`/`agent_id` (`childMarker`) to its payloads and skips `fetchHandoff`; the router treats an omp child's SessionEnd like OpenCode's (page and summary, no baton). A person typing into an agent-opened session makes it theirs.
  - **Repair, once per launch** (`MemoryUpkeep.repair`, idempotent): session pages with a junk title are rewritten through `memory_write_page` with the session (keeps `session_id`/`agent`; precondition = the page version just read; `/admin/write-page` would drop that frontmatter) and the open handoffs a child left are cancelled. Counts go to `gui-runtime.jsonl` (`upkeep-retitle`).
  - **Learning**: ai-memory's own consolidation needs an LLM provider on the server and OMPdesk keeps the server provider-less, so durable pages (decisions, `_rules`, gotchas, procedures) come from a one-shot `omp -p --model pi/smol --no-tools --no-session --no-extensions` in an EMPTY temp folder (a repo folder would load its AGENTS.md: 157k tokens, $0.64 a call instead of $0.015). It reads only what a PERSON typed (agent-written prompts never) and answers JSON; `parseProposals` refuses unknown kinds, thin bodies, confidence under 0.75, uncited or already-used titles, and the app creates pages with `create_only` and the session as evidence. Schedule: a conversation with ≥ 6 new person prompts (`DISTILL_MIN_PROMPTS`) and 10 minutes quiet (`UPKEEP_IDLE_MS`), one run per hour (`UPKEEP_MIN_GAP_MS`), six per day (`UPKEEP_DAILY_MAX`), state in `<userData>/ai-memory/upkeep.json`; Settings › Memory › Overview "Learn from conversations" (pref `aiMemoryLearn`, default on).
  - Tests: `memory-prompt.test.ts`, `retitle.test.ts`, `distill*.test.ts`, `upkeep.test.ts`, `e2e/memory-upkeep.e2e.ts` (fake server project `gamma`, `FAKE_AI_MEMORY_UPKEEP=1`).
- **Cost and privacy** (3.1.1): +3.5k tokens of MCP schemas and +0.36k of skills per session (prompt-cached), +0.4k at the first prompt only with an open handoff; idle server 0.0% CPU, 39 MB resident without embeddings. Prompts and tool results are scrubbed of vendor keys (`sk-`, `ghp_`), bearer tokens, private keys, `*_KEY=…` env shapes and URL passwords before storage (tested); free text such as "my password is …" is stored as typed; assistant replies are not recorded; `[capture] ignore_paths` in `.ai-memory.toml` covers file and shell tools only.
- **Tests**: `src/main/ai-memory/*.test.ts` (staging, selection, client, service with a fake runner and fake unit: off never lists or applies an uninstall-all, the root is loaded only while on, ownership, other clients, purge, the terminal button, the migration; plus `terminal`, `unit`, `root`, IPC; on macOS the real codesign refuses `/usr/bin/true`, an ad-hoc copy named `ai-memory` and an ad-hoc re-signed copy of the synced release); `src/main/setup/runner.test.ts` (an isolated command's detached descendant holds none of the supervisor's pipes; argv quoting); `sidecar-factory.test.ts` and `schedules.test.ts` (the `-e` seams).
  - `e2e/ai-memory.e2e.ts` on the stateful fake binary `e2e/fixture/fake-ai-memory.ts` (a planted Claude Code wiring survives on/off, root, terminal button, delete-all, migration, Settings, panel). CI: `gh workflow run ci.yml -f win_e2e=true -f win_e2e_grep=ai-memory`; `-f installer_check=true` for the packaged setup.
  - `e2e/first-launch-window.e2e.ts` (`@smoke`, Windows: the packaged app sets the real service up; ⌘Q's quit and an update's quit must close the app's pipes within 30 s and leave no supervisor, `ai-memory` command or omp of OMPdesk's running, while the service may stay; ci.yml's win-e2e packages `dist/win-unpacked` for it; `-f win_e2e_grep=first-launch`).
  - `e2e/packaged-ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`@smoke`, macOS: the packaged `dist/mac-arm64` app, or `OMP_GUI_E2E_APP`, in a private HOME and userData stages the re-signed binary, reaches `ready` with its real launchd service, creates the root and logs `launch`/`binary`/`phase: ready`; skipped for an ad-hoc package and while the user's own label or port 49374 is in use; ends with `uninstall --apply --yes` in the private HOME).
  - Opt-in `e2e/real-ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`: real release in a temp HOME with its real service, real omp reads the root's MCP entry, two sessions in one folder, the second gets the first's handoff, off removes the launchd service).

### Sidebar families and subagent titles

- Files: `layout/sidebar/SidebarSubagents.tsx` (group, chip), `SidebarFamilyRow.tsx` (one-line row), `sidebar-family.ts` (pure rules), `stores/sidebar-subagents.ts` (open state).
- **Family** = everything derived from a session, shown together under its row, one indent level in: its subagents (the tab runtime's roster) and the sessions its agent opened with `ompdesk_session_open`, in a worktree or not, whatever their folder.
- **Provenance**: `AgentControlRuntime.#spawn` calls `SidecarPool.setTabOpener(tabId, callerTabId)` (not for an Autopilot child, which keeps its run's row). The tab entry carries `openedBy` (opener's tab id) and `openedBySession` (its session file) on `IpcTabInfo`; the saved tab layout keeps the file as `PersistedTabDescriptor.openedBy` (a restored tab's id is new; the file finds the opener; the opener's tab closing leaves the file). The renderer matches by tab id first, then by file (`openerOfSessions`): an opened session whose opener is listed leaves its folder's group (the "Parallel work" group lists only sessions without a live opener), one opened by an opened session goes under the first of them, a cycle nests nothing.
- **Row**: ONE line (`.omp-family-row`, 28 px; `--sc-row-touch` 36 px on a phone): 16 px kind glyph (subagent `Bot`, worktree session `GitBranch`, plain session `MessageSquare`), title, one status token (a still accent dot while running, waiting ⚠, done ✓, failed ✕, paused) and age; status in words, folder, model and progress are the tooltip. A subagent's title follows "Subagent titles" below; an opened session's is `agentTaskTitle`/`sessionDisplayTitle` (the `Title:` line else its first line, via `assignmentHeadline`/`stripTitlePrefix` in `subagent-title-contracts.ts`). The parent row's chip reads "🤖5 · ⑂4" with the running count as a filled pill. A phone bounds hit areas with `--hit-gap` (`mobile.css`).
- **A family is folded by default and NEVER opens or folds by itself.** WHY (v0.38.1, phone): every follow-up message to a subagent is a new run, so the old open-on-start edge (`noteFamilyActive`) re-opened families over the person's fold; it was removed with fold-on-finish. Running, finishing or starting children change only the chip (running count = filled pill; warning tint `data-family-waiting`, `sidebar.family.waiting` in its summary while a child waits for the person).
  - The only thing a folded family shows unasked is a child that waits for an answer or approval (`waiting`: a stale registration, or an opened session `awaitingInput`): that row shows alone under the folded parent (`data-sidebar-surfaced`, flat, no connector for the others) and leaves when answered; the store's `expanded` is untouched.
  - The person's own toggle (chip, session row click, ←/→, "expand/collapse all", the dock's reveal) is the only writer of `expanded`, remembered per session in memory; with no child the entry is dropped.
- **Finished children are never listed** (the user's rule: a finished child is meaningless in the sidebar). A family shows only children that are live or need a look: running, waiting, failed or stale, or an opened session with work left to review (`problem`: failed ending, unmerged commits, uncommitted work). A finished subagent (completed/aborted/cancelled, `isFoldedSubagent`) or a finished opened session that landed clean (`status` `done`, `isShownOpened`) has NO row and NO "n done" fold (no `sidebar.family.done` string, no `data-family-done`). The chip counts only shown children (`familyCounts`) and is absent when none show; a family left with nothing is dropped from the store. Nothing is deleted: a finished subagent stays in its session's Subagent panel and the work board; a finished opened session stays in the session picker/search. "Hide this session's subagents" hides only the subagents.
- **Opened sessions are children with the same lifecycle and visibility as subagents.** One derived status per opened session (`openedChildStatus` in `sidebar-family.ts`, a rule table over `ChildFacts`: waiting, running, problem = finished with an error/abort ending or unmerged/uncommitted work, done = finished; `CHILD_TOKEN` maps it to the row token, a problem is ✕) feeds the row token, chip counts and fold. A `done` one is not listed (`FamilyOpened.status`); running, waiting, problem and not-yet-run ones stay rows.
  - A session main closed after its work landed has no tab, so provenance is pref `agentClosedChildren` (`ClosedChild`: file, opener's file, title, folder; read by `stores/closed-children.ts`, `openerOfSessions(…, closedOpeners)`): not listed (done); the record still finds its opener, so a reopened one keeps nesting and a stored one opens from the session picker (in `cwd` = the checkout it came from when its worktree was removed).
  - A session no tab holds is never a row of its own or a "Parallel work" group. Its opener is found, in order, by the tab's `openedBySession` file (the opener needs no tab: a stored opener row carries the family too), the closed record, then the agent brief in its indexed first message (`agentMessageSender` reads `from` of `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">`; `briefOpenerOf`: the newest session with that title created before the child; an Autopilot child keeps its run's row whatever its brief says).
  - One without an opener (none listed from record or brief, or only its `omp/agent/…` worktree says an agent opened it) is listed only while it needs a look (`SidebarLooseChildren`, `data-sidebar-loose-children`: plain family-style rows closing its project's main-checkout group; the recent list has the same at its end; a project with nothing else to show has no group); a finished one is not listed; a live tab keeps its row.
  - The row title is `sessionDisplayTitle`, else the tab's `task` (`IpcTabInfo.task`, the task's `Title:` line), else the closed record's title, so an opened session never reads "(untitled)" before its index entry has a first message.
  - The same children are on the work board ("Opened sessions" section, `opened-children.ts` + `hooks/use-opened-children.ts`: title, status token, current step and clock from the review item main pushes, branch and merge state; waiting first with the warning tint; status only, no buttons, a click focuses the child), counted by the phone strip (⑂ count with running/waiting in the label; the expanded panel is the board), the composer's background cue (running ones), the dock island ("Sessions" segment) and the turn-resume log (`opened`). The board has no "Needs review" section and no Review/Approve/Discard/dismiss action (`needsReview` is gone): the opener's agent reviews and lands its children with `ompdesk_compare`/`_merge`/`_session_stop`, and a child that needs the person is a waiting row and a notification.
- Tests: `SidebarSubagents.test.tsx` (no auto-open, the waiting child alone, remembered toggle, no done row), `sidebar-family.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` (nesting by opener, folded by default, finished children not listed), `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `tab-layout.test.ts` (provenance persistence), `touch-targets.test.ts` (row sizes); `e2e/family-fold.e2e.ts` on the dev harness (phone and desktop, `OMP_GUI_FAMILY_FOLD=<dir>` writes captures).

#### Subagent titles

- `subagentPrimaryLabel` (`panels/subagent-graph.ts`), store rules in `stores/subagents.ts`, `unwrapStockPrompt` in `resources/gui-bridge/modes/subagent-title-contracts.ts`. A subagent's row, panel header and board card read the `Title:` line of its ORIGINAL assignment, else stock's own label (`description`), else the first line of that assignment, else the role name; never a later message.
- Stock's frames differ per run (`task/executor.ts`, `modes/rpc/rpc-subagents.ts`). The first run's progress carries `task` = the assignment wrapped by `prompts/system/subagent-user-prompt.md` ("Complete assignment thoroughly:" + blank line) and `assignment` = the bare text. Every later run reports its own message as `task`, NO `assignment`, no label (stock's `handleProgress` then overwrites the roster's `assignment` with undefined): **(a)** a follow-up to an idle subagent (`attachIrcWakeTurnMonitor`; `task` is the plain follow-up text), **(b)** revival after relaunch (`restoreSession` → `runSubagentFollowUpTurn`; `task` is the wrapped original).
- So: `assignment` is sticky in the store (a frame without one keeps the row's); `unwrapStockPrompt(task)` recovers it from (b) (store and label); a plain `task` is never a label; a roster snapshot after a follow-up (no assignment, no label) reads the first user message of the subagent's transcript once (`recoverAssignment`, `get_subagent_messages` then `get_subagent_transcript`). Pinned by `stores/subagent-titles.test.ts` with stock-shaped frames.

### Work board and work card

- **Work board** (`layout/work-board/WorkBoard.tsx`, rules in `work-board.ts`, look in `styles/work-board.css`, design record `docs/work-board-design.md`; mounted per session pane by `SplitWorkspace`): a pane with at least 300 px (`WORK_BOARD_MIN_SPARE`) beside its transcript column (`workBoardFits`, measured by ResizeObserver; constants pinned to components.css by `work-board.test.ts`) docks this session's live state in that margin.
  - Content, in order: one status line with the todo progress bar; waiting questions/approvals first (warning wash; ⌥B `board.focus` jumps to them; the only live region); the turn's last steps (turn meter's tok/s beside elapsed time); running subagents (model mark, step, tokens) and background jobs; todos (finished folded); goal meters; the last turn's duration and +/− once idle. Section folds persist (pref `workBoardCollapsed`). The head also holds `AccountLimit.tsx` (the subscription account in use and its tightest usage window from `get_usage` via `useUsageReports`; warning from 80 %, danger at the limit; hidden without usage windows); rows leave through `usePresence`.
  - With a section the pane carries `data-work-board`: transcript scroller and composer shift left by `--omp-board-reserve`, and `data-work-board-running` / `-todo` hide the dock strips (`[data-workspace-island]`, `[data-dock-card="todo"]`) that say the same. It reads the existing per-tab stores and `get_jobs`/`get_goal`, derives nothing, never shows beside the side panel, and is off with pref `workBoard` (default on; Settings › Appearance, command palette, its own ✕).
  - Cost rules (`e2e/flow-performance.e2e.ts` `daily` flow): its only running animation is the head pulse (rows fade in/out once, then stay still; each animated row cost ~2 % of a GPU core while streaming); `useNowTick` stops while the window is hidden; `get_usage` and `get_jobs` run one poll per tab via `hooks/use-tab-poll.ts` (each joining surface reads once on mount): `use-usage-reports.ts` serves the board's account row, sidebar usage lines, Stats › Usage, Settings model tables and accounts and the usage-limit notice; `use-background-jobs.ts` serves the board, dock island and Background tasks view.
  - Tests: `WorkBoard.test.tsx`, `work-board.test.ts`, `use-usage-reports.test.tsx`, `now-tick.test.tsx`, e2e `work-board.e2e.ts` (fake omp's `fixture:board`).
  - **Where it cannot dock (phone, portrait tablet)** the status strip under the header (`WorkBoardChip`) expands in place: a tap grows an inline panel (`data-work-strip-panel`, wrapper `.omp-work-strip`, absolutely placed under the strip) over the top of the transcript, which is neither pushed nor dimmed: no Sheet, no scrim or backdrop, so the transcript stays visible and takes taps below. The panel holds the board itself (waiting ask with its jump to the card first, steps, running subagents, jobs, todos, goal), capped at `50dvh`, scrolling inside (`.omp-work-strip-body`), inset like the strip for safe areas and scrollbar gutter.
    - Collapses on a second tap of the strip, a swipe up (≥ 32 px) on the strip or the 16 px grip at the panel's foot, the back gesture (`useBackLayer`), a pointerdown anywhere outside (document listener, so the tap still reaches what it hit), or a tap on a board row (`button.omp-work-board-row`; its action runs first). Section fold toggles and review's approve/discard keep it open. The strip stays and its chevron flips (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). Nothing is remembered: every mount starts collapsed. Motion is height (`omp-strip-in`/`-out`, exit keeps the panel mounted 160 ms) and the chevron's transform only; reduced motion and `html[data-gpu="software"]` make both instant. Pinned by `WorkBoardChip.test.tsx` and the `mobile-shell` e2e (panel under the header, ≤ 50 % high, hit test below it lands on the transcript, second tap and outside tap collapse).
- **Work card head, findings and steps** (`chat/ExecutionGroup.tsx`, pure rules in `chat/work/work-checks.ts`, `work-findings.ts`, `work-steps.ts`; design record `docs/work-card-design.md`):
  - A finished card's fact line names what the run's shell commands checked instead of counting them. `checkKind` reads the command's own words (test runners, `tsc`/`check:types`, biome/eslint/ruff, `build`); the last run of each kind decides (`summarizeChecks`: "128 tests passed", "types OK", "build failed"); a non-zero `details.exitCode` fails even without stock's error flag; interrupted and unanswered runs are skipped and still count as commands. The test count is the runner's last printed summary (`testCounts`: vitest, jest, bun, pytest, cargo, go); other commands keep "N commands".
  - Under the head (LiveStepPeek's `findings` slot, finished cards only so streaming never reflows) one row of chips (class `omp-work-finding`; `omp-work-chip` is the composer's work chip), derived by `deriveFindings` from the ledger alone, no model call: **Error** (`data-work-chip="error"`, danger; the failing check's cause via `firstErrorLine` over `WorkCommandOutcome.text`, the last 8,000 characters kept only for checks and failed commands, ANSI and runner chrome skipped; else a failed non-check command's `firstFailure`; else the word "Error" for a failed call of any other tool), **Tests ✓ N** (`tests`, last test run passed), **Open Changes** (`changes`, `setPanelTab("diff")`), **Preview** (`preview`, a bash result `devServerUrl` (`lib/panel-auto-open.ts`), kept as `WorkCommandOutcome.previewUrl`; `openPreview`) and a copy icon (`CopyButton`, `workSummaryText`: head facts and time, cause, changed files with +/-, checks, preview address).
  - Error and Tests open the steps and show their call: `show(toolCallId)` sets a `StepReveal` read through `StepRevealContext`; `revealStep` (`work/reveal-step.ts`) finds the card by `data-tool-call-id` (`ToolCard`'s root; the ledger carries each main-agent command's key as `WorkCommandOutcome.toolCallId`, a subagent's none), opens it and scrolls to the middle, retrying for 2 s because a big card mounts in slices.
  - The opened finished card (`StepGroups.tsx`, `groupSteps`; a running card keeps `ProcessMessages`, one bubble per message, so the live list never rearranges; `transcriptDetail` full mode has no work card) lines consecutive calls of one stock intent (`ToolCallContent.intent`, then the arguments' `intent`/`i`; a generic "Running requested command" names nothing) under one closed row ("Checking the types · 3 steps"), folds consecutive collapsible reads into `ReadGroupCard`, and every call keeps its own `ToolCard`. A group (or read group, `forceOpen`) holding a failed call opens by itself and again when a finding reveals a call inside it; only thinking may sit between two calls of one intent.
  - Pinned by `work-checks.test.ts`, `work-findings.test.ts`, `work-steps.test.ts`, `work-replay.test.ts` (recorded `real-advisor-frames.json` and stock's bash envelope through ledger and rules), `ExecutionGroup.checks.test.tsx`, `ExecutionGroup.chips.test.tsx`, `StepGroups.test.tsx`, `e2e/event-replay.e2e.ts`. Captures: `OMP_GUI_WORK_CARD_SHOTS=<dir>` with `e2e/work-card-captures.e2e.ts` (pass and `OMP_GUI_TEST_WORK_TYPES_FAIL=1`, en/ko × light/dark, wide/narrow, card opened) and `e2e/work-card-phone.e2e.ts` (paired phone page at 390), both on the fake omp's `OMP_GUI_TEST_WORK_RICH=1` variant; `OMP_GUI_WORK_CARD_OUT`/`_LABEL` capture another build as the before.

### Split view and picking a session

- Files: `stores/tabs.ts`, rules in `shared/split-layout.ts`. Main owns tabs and the pool; the renderer owns what the window shows.
- A window shows one session alone or one split of 2–4 panes (`split`), and remembers the other splits it has shown (`rememberedSplits`: layouts only, no focus). A session is in at most one split, on screen or remembered.
- Three rules, all in `viewAfterPick`; every way of picking a session goes through `switchTab`: ① a pane of the split on screen only takes focus, the layout stays; ② a session outside the split leaves it and shows alone, full width, and the split on screen is remembered; it never replaces or opens into a pane; ③ a member of a remembered split brings that split back exactly as it was (sessions, positions, sizes) with the picked session focused, and the split that was on screen is remembered in turn.
- Picks: sidebar rows, ⌘1–⌘9, ⇧⌘[ ⇧⌘] / ⌘PageUp/PageDown when no split is on screen (with one they cycle its panes), tab overview, ⌃⇥ switcher, notification/prompt-card focus. The session picker (⇧⌘F) and the sidebar's stored-session rows open a session no tab holds as a new tab: a pick of an outside session (`switchSessionNow` never re-roots a pane's tab while a split shows; `activeTabUntouched` is false then).
- The only edits of the layout: dropping a session on a pane edge (`splitTab`, drag-to-split, row menu's Split right/bottom) and the menu's Exit split view. A dropped session leaves the split it was remembered in; at four panes it replaces the pane under the pointer and that one leaves; Exit split view forgets the split. Closing a tab (`closeTab`, which a deleted session's tab goes through) and removing a pane drop the session from its split; a split left with one session dissolves.
- Sidebar: every row whose tab is a pane of the split on screen has `data-active`; the focused pane's row adds `data-pane-focus` (a quiet colorless edge, only while a split shows).
- Persistence: the renderer sends `rememberedSplits` with every `tabs.setView` (`tabs.setActive` only while neither side remembers any). Main keeps them per window (`#rememberedByWindow`, pruned on release, never overlapping what is on screen) and saves them as `splits` next to `split` in the tab layout (`PersistedTabLayout`, leaves are tab indices); `GET_TABS` returns each remembered split once, on its first tab (`rememberedSplit`).
- Tests: `shared/split-layout.test.ts`, `tabs.test.tsx`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `tab-layout.test.ts`, `sidecar-pool.test.ts`; e2e `split-select.e2e.ts` (3 panes: all selected, outside pick solo, member restores, relaunch), `split-drop`, `desktop-tabs`, `workbench-shortcuts`.

### Preview: agent browsing and agent visuals

- **Agent browsing** (Preview toolbar robot button, local tabs only, off until the user clicks it): the tab's bridge (`resources/gui-bridge/browser-relay.ts`) starts stock's own `omp browser-relay` server (`tools/browser/relay/server.ts`) on a free 127.0.0.1 port with a random extension token and holds `browser.relay`/`browser.relayUrl` as session runtime overrides, so stock's browser tool runs in relay mode. Main joins as the relay's "Chrome extension" (`src/main/preview/agent-relay.ts`, stock's `relay/protocol.ts`) over the preview page's `webContents.debugger`, offering that one page, never the app window (which keeps no remote-debugging port).
  - Electron's per-page debugger is not confined to its page (Electron 36: `Target.getTargets` lists the app window and `Target.attachToTarget` onto it answers `Runtime.evaluate`; only `attachToBrowserTarget` is "Not allowed"). So the relay forwards an **allowlist** only: the page domains stock's puppeteer uses (`Page`, `Runtime`, `DOM`, `Input`, `Network`, `Emulation`, … never `Browser`, `SystemInfo`, `Tethering`, `Tracing`, `Memory`), and from `Target` just `setAutoAttach` and `detachFromTarget`. `Tracing` is browser-wide (records the app's other windows), so stock's `tab.traceStart()` fails by design.
  - Also refused: `Page.navigate` off http(s)/about:blank, `Page.close`/`crash`, downloads, `DOM.setFileInputFiles`, `Page.setInterceptFileChooserDialog`, `Input.setInterceptDrags`, an `Input.dispatchDragEvent` whose `data` carries `files` or a `file:` URL (Electron 36's debugger turns `files` into a real dropped File from the local path), a `Network.loadNetworkResource` whose `url` is not http(s) (Electron 36 already says "Unsupported URL scheme" for `file:`; the rule keeps local files out whatever a later Electron does), and `Security.setIgnoreCertificateErrors`/`setOverrideCertificateErrors`/`handleCertificateError`.
  - `Page.navigateToHistoryEntry` is allowed only after the relay reads `Page.getNavigationHistory` and the target entry's URL passes the same http(s)/about:blank rule, so `tab.back()`/`tab.forward()` work. The relay addresses only sessions this page's own debugger announced with `Target.attachedToTarget` (its frames and workers, never a `page`/`tab`/`browser` target); the page keeps its partition, sandbox and no-preload rules.
  - A banner shows the agent's last CDP method; Take over (or a tab switch, closing the panel, the session ending) detaches the debugger, closes the socket and stops the relay. `e2e/preview-agent.e2e.ts` drives it with stock's relay server and puppeteer-core from the installed omp: back/forward work; `Target.getTargets`/`attachToTarget`, `Tracing.start`, a file drag and a `file:` resource load are refused.
- **Agent visuals** (`src/main/preview/visuals.ts`, `visual-navigation.ts`, `page-scripts.ts`; contract `src/shared/preview.ts`): the `gui_visualize` host tool (registered with `gui_terminal_read`, local tabs only) shows a project `.html`/`.svg`/image/`.txt` file, inline HTML the agent wrote or an http(s) page as a Preview tab of the calling tab, and keeps a version per change (`<userData>/visuals/<id>/v<N>.<ext>`, 20 per visual, 100 visuals in `history.json`, Page options › All visuals…). `gui_visualize open` with an existing `visual` id moves that record to the new target.
  - Sessions: each window's preview has two in-memory sessions reused by every preview host of that window, storage cleared when a host is disposed: `web` (`preview-<win>`) for every page the user, a popup, the agent's browser or a web visual browses, and `visual` (`preview-visual-<win>`) for file and inline visuals alone. Those load from `omp-visual://v<N>-<id>/…`, registered on the visual session only: the entry from its snapshot, other files from the entry file's own folder (the project folder when the entry sits at its root) by known asset type, never hidden paths or anything outside it. A page stays in the session it was made in (`PreviewPageState.session`): a visual opens in a visual-session page (an empty web tab is replaced, a web tab is never reused), an address typed on a visual's tab opens a new web tab, and the agent's browser never takes a visual's page.
  - Egress: every response (404 included) carries a CSP (`VISUAL_CSP`: `default-src 'none'` with each allowed kind named, `connect-src 'self' data: blob:`, `form-action 'none'`, no frames or objects) so the page cannot send what it reads off its origin by `fetch`, `sendBeacon`, `<a ping>`, WebSocket, image, CSS, prefetch, form or frame. What CSP does not cover the host refuses (Security #3): whatever a visual's page starts itself (link, `location`, form, meta refresh, frame load, redirect) goes only to another version of the same visual or `about:blank`/`about:srcdoc` (`visualMayNavigate`); popups never open; downloads are dropped silently; WebRTC runs with `disable_non_proxied_udp`; the visual session's proxy is a dead `http://127.0.0.1:1` with `<-loopback>`, so TURN over TCP and any other socket go nowhere.
  - DNS prefetch, which no CSP or proxy stops, is off app-wide: `index.ts` adds `dnsPrefetchingEnabled=false` to `--blink-settings` (merged with any given at launch) before ready; Blink applies it after each page's preferences in every frame and `x-dns-prefetch-control: on` cannot undo it.
  - A web address refused right after a real click or key on the page (the C143 gesture window) shows in full in a notice whose "Open in new tab" loads it in a web tab, only when the user picks it.
  - `e2e/preview-visual-egress.e2e.ts` pins all of it against a 127.0.0.1 sentinel (TCP and UDP) that must hear nothing and the visual session's net-log, which must hold no host resolution the page asked for.
