  - The page keeps the Preview's sandbox (no preload, permission-less sessions). Comment on an element / region, pins, parent element and CSS variable tweaks run in an isolated world (`PAGE_WORLD`) on trusted events and go to the composer as one message; `choose` returns a `data-choice` click only when trusted: a real mouse/key release on that page within 1 s and neither the agent's relay nor any debugger holds the page.

### SSH tools on this computer

- `src/main/ssh/`, contract `src/shared/ssh-tools.ts`, Settings › SSH: port forwards for a saved host (`ssh -N -L 127.0.0.1:<local>:127.0.0.1:<remote>`, saved in `<userData>/ssh-forwards.json`, started again at launch), the server's listening ports every 5 s (`SSH_PORT_POLL_SECONDS`), ssh-agent/key state, and a reconnect with growing delay until the chosen grace time (1 min–7 days, default 1 h).
- Every such ssh runs under the setup supervisor with `-F <userData>/ssh/config`, whose first `Host *` block pins OMPdesk's own `known_hosts` with `StrictHostKeyChecking ask` and no connection sharing, then includes `~/.ssh/config`. OpenSSH hands `-F` to every ProxyJump hop, so each hop's key is checked too.
- Host-key, password, passphrase and code questions come through `SSH_ASKPASS` (`askpass.ts`: a Node helper run by the app binary, a per-process token over a local socket) into `SshPromptDialog`; answers are never stored and expire after 2 min. A changed host key stops the forward until the user forgets the saved key.
- The agent's own ssh tool stays stock's. SSH server windows (a headless OMPdesk on the server) are out of scope: OMPdesk ships for macOS and Windows only, and remote devices cover running work on another of the user's computers.

### Built-in MCP (REA)

- `resources/omp-builtin/rea/.mcp.json` starts the dependency-free Node `rea-relay.mjs` via stock's `${OMP_PLUGIN_ROOT}` substitution, outside asar, as a directory `-e` on every eligible spawn (REA root = `resources/omp-builtin/rea/`; `resources/omp-builtin/` itself is OMPdesk's own root, see OMPdesk control tools). The relay runs pinned REA (`rea/rea-config.json` is the sole version pin), exposes 15 unchanged entry-tool schemas (`curatedTools`) plus `rea_catalog` / `rea_call`, and keeps the full catalog on demand instead of adding 207 xd:// mount-notice lines to each session.
- The same root carries REA's `reverse-engineer-anything` skill in `rea/skills/` (stock loads an extension root's `skills/`), so it comes and goes with the server. It is vendored from the pinned release plus an OMPdesk section (mounted vs `rea_call` tools, no `rea setup`) by `bun run sync:rea-skill`, which every version bump runs (`packaging-config.test.ts` fails on a stale copy).
- Node/npx must satisfy REA's engines. Pref `builtinMcp.rea` (default on) governs tabs this machine spawns or hosts. stdin EOF kills the child process tree. Never write the user's MCP config.

### Built-in pstack engineering mode

- Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation, pref `pstack` (`PSTACK_PREF`), default **on**; contract `src/shared/pstack.ts`, row `settings/PstackRow.tsx`. `resources/omp-builtin/pstack/` is a translated copy of [pstack](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/main/pstack) (MIT, Lauren Tan; pin `pstack-config.json`: commit `0b8e952…`, 0.15.26) loaded as a directory `-e` beside the REA root only while the switch is on (`sidecar-factory.ts` `builtinMcp`, from the next spawn).
- Content: `skills/` (principles, playbooks under `poteto-mode/playbooks/`, workflow skills; every one `disable-model-invocation`, so hidden from the model's skill list and read as `skill://<name>`), `agents/` (`pstack-agent`, the wrapper every playbook delegate uses, and `comment-sicko`), the hand-written `adapter.md` (Cursor/Claude names → omp `task`/`ask`/`todo`, `ompdesk_session_open`/`compare`/`merge`, model roles `@task`/`@slow`/`@smol`, `~/.omp/agent/sessions` transcripts, the routing gate) and `pstack-manifest.json`.
- **Never add a `plugin.json` there**: omp would class the folder an Agent Plugins root and drop 54 of the skills for their frontmatter. `tdd`/`teach` are `pstack-tdd`/`pstack-teach`, so no stock or user skill is shadowed. Left out as Cursor-only: `setup-pstack`, `poteto-help`, `make-bot-ui`, Bugbot triage, the `orchestrate` playbook, the `watch-pr`/`orch` tools.
- Everything except `pstack-config.json`, `adapter.md`, `agents/pstack-agent.md` and `README.md` is generated by `bun run sync:pstack` (`scripts/sync-pstack.ts`: clone at the pin or `PSTACK_SRC=<clone>`, copy, a rule table in which every rule must match at least once, so a reworded upstream line fails the sync; `-- --check` lists newer upstream commits) and pinned by `src/main/pstack/pack-drift.test.ts` (sha256 manifest, no `plugin.json`, names and links, no leftover Cursor term, section size cap, tool names).
- The always-on part is one `registerPromptSection` section (`resources/gui-bridge/modes/pstack.ts` + `pstack-core.ts`; texts generated into `pstack-text.ts` from `adapter.md` and the translated `poteto-mode` skill: about 20 KB / 7.3k tokens): static text for the prompt cache, main agent only, opening with the precedence repo rules > user settings > a skill the user invoked > the pack, then a routing gate (a trivial request gets a plain answer; anything else reads the matching playbook first and copies its steps into `todo`).
- Off for subagents (they get `pstack-agent`), Plan mode, a chat session without a project (`OMP_GUI_PSTACK=0` at spawn) and the switch off. An Autopilot run gets the 2 KB `PSTACK_AUTOPILOT_SECTION` instead (its protocol and HARD LIMITS win; bug-fix, hillclimb, autonomous-run, session-pickup, pause-safely and prove-it-works, no shipping or PR playbooks). Solo delegation swaps the delegation paragraph.
- Tests: `bridge-pstack.test.ts` (gating table and `install` on a fake `pi`), `e2e/pstack.e2e.ts` (fake omp whose `get_skills` and context inspector read the `-e` roots, real gating under `OMP_GUI_TEST_PSTACK=1`), opt-in `e2e/real-pstack.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, `smol` role: a bug-fix prompt must read `bug-fix.md` and copy its steps into `todo`, a greeting stays plain, the switch-off control never reads the playbook; a small model sometimes skips the playbook on a one-line bug, so it asks one follow in three runs).

## Models, accounts and held stock defaults

### Desktop slot
- One session at a time drives the desktop. Files: contract `resources/gui-bridge/desktop-slot-contracts.ts`; bridge `desktop-slot.ts` + `desktop-slot-core.ts`; main `src/main/desktop-slot.ts` + `desktop-slot-host.ts` + `local/desktop-slot.ts`; renderer `stores/desktop-slot.ts`, `DesktopSlotIndicator.tsx`, the waiting line in `ToolCard.tsx`; capability `desktopSlot`.
- WHY: each omp has its own `ComputerSupervisor`; stock's native layer only refuses input while another operation owns focus ("no input was sent"). It neither queues nor spans a session's observe → act → verify, and nothing crosses processes. So **main** owns one slot per computer and the bridge patches `ComputerSupervisor.prototype.run` once (feature-detected; without `run`/`revokeControl` the slot is `unavailable` in capabilities/health and stock behaviour stays).
- A `snapshot.readOnly` run (screenshots, `capabilities`) never waits. The first acting run takes the slot (`desktop_slot_acquire` → `desktop_slot_bridge`) and keeps it until `agent_end`, `DESKTOP_SLOT_IDLE_MS` (20 s) without a computer run, Stop, abort, tab close/crash, or lease expiry (`DESKTOP_SLOT_LEASE_MS` 120 s without `desktop_slot_touch`).
- Waiters queue FIFO. An acting run that had to wait does **not** run once granted: it returns `DESKTOP_SLOT_STALE_NOTICE` so the agent re-screenshots and repeats.
- **Handoff**: abort the holder's in-flight input run, await stock `ComputerController.revokeControl()` on every controller the bridge saw, only then grant the next session.
- **Never handed on unconfirmed.** A `revokeControl()` that rejects (holder's acting runs get "input is blocked") or is silent for `DESKTOP_SLOT_REVOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) leaves the holder `revoke-failed` (`DesktopSlotState.holder.revokeFailed`): nobody is granted, main retries every `DESKTOP_SLOT_REVOKE_RETRY_MS` (5 s; a late answer counts), the indicator offers "다시 시도" (`desktop_slot_retry`) and "그 세션 중지" (`desktop_slot_kill`: abort + `restart()` of its omp, SIGTERM only).
- Freed only by a confirmed revoke, a confirmed exit of the holder's process (host re-reads `whenExited()` while the sidecar is ready; a failed grant is not a crash), or a kill whose process exited within `KILL_CONFIRM_MS` (5 s). A bridge `desktop_slot_release` follows only a confirmed revoke.
- UI: app-wide "화면 사용 중: <title> · 대기 N" with Stop (`desktop_slot_stop`: aborts the holder's turn, hands off); a waiting `eval` card says "화면 차례를 기다리는 중 (N번째)".
- Hosted tabs for a paired device join this slot; tabs on a remote host use that host's slot (main skips `sidecar.device` tabs). Autopilot never takes it (its `eval` is off). No "run marked done" release (stock has no marker).
- Tests: `desktop-slot.test.ts`, `desktop-slot-host.test.ts`, `bridge-desktop-slot.test.ts` (fail-first: rejecting revoke, silent revoke, holder death after failed revoke, kill), `e2e/desktop-slot.e2e.ts`.

### Account rotation
- Settings › Models & accounts pref `accountRotation` (`quota` default = stock's usage ranking | `even`; `src/shared/account-rotation.ts`, `providers/RotationModeControl.tsx`). Stock has no switch: each spawn passes `OMP_GUI_ACCOUNT_ROTATION` and the shared ledger `<userData>/account-rotation.json` (`OMP_GUI_ACCOUNT_ROTATION_STATE`); a change or bridge connect pushes `account_rotation_set {mode}` to every live local sidecar and every tab this machine hosts (`LocalMachine.setAccountRotation`, `src/main/account-rotation.ts`). A paired host's tabs follow the host's pref (`host.accountRotation.get|set`).
- **Even**: the bridge's `account-rotation.ts` (`before_agent_start`) gives an account-less session the unblocked account handed out least recently across all local omps, as an automatic warm pin (`authStorage.sessions.pin(…, {restoredAtMs})`). A session keeps it until blocked (idle pin renewed, not re-ranked); stock's 429 blocking/failover is untouched.
- **Quota re-ranks at turn start.** WHY: stock re-ranks an automatic pin only when cold (Claude: 60 min idle), blocked or spent, and `affinity.record` refreshes last-use on every request, so a session used without a 60-min gap stayed on its first account for days (subagents inherit the parent's pin via `sessions.inherit`). `rerankBeforePrompt` (`account-rotation-core.ts`) runs in the same hook for the main agent only (`ctx.agent.kind === "main"`; subagents keep the spawn copy), never mid-turn.
- It asks stock, not our own policy: the account stock's selector would give a pinless session now (`authStorage.keys.getWithCredential(provider, <stand-in id>, {…, recordAffinity: false})`). It moves the pin (automatic warm pin, `restoredAtMs: now`) only when ALL hold, else stock decides: >1 account; pin exists and is warm; pin is automatic (read via the `include(provider, explicit)` callback of `sessions.inherit` with a stand-in target returning `false`; no provenance = left alone); session unrestricted (`sessions.permits`) and no account has a stock policy (`oauth.policy`): pools/policies are the user's choice; pin not blocked; both accounts measured and stock's comparator (`orderUsageRankedCandidates` over `RankedOAuthCandidate` rows from both `usage.report`s, mirroring `#rankOAuthSelections`) still ranks the other first after the pin's required drain is multiplied by `RERANK_DRAIN_MARGIN` (2).
- The margin is OMPdesk's only addition (stock compares drain at 1e-6 and every switch rewrites the provider's per-account prompt cache). Only the plain drain order gets it; stock tiers (blocked, spent, 85% 5h demotion, unmeasured) move a pin at once; a round trip needs a 4x swing. Proper fix belongs in stock.
- Tests: `src/main/bridge-account-rotation-stock.test.ts` on the real omp (2.7x moves, 1.9x never, 2.1x does, no way back at 1.9x, hot 5h tier, explicit/cold/subagent, inheritance), rules in `bridge-account-rotation.test.ts`; internals in `capabilities.ts` (`accountRotation`) and `health.ts` (`auth/rank`).

### Provider sign-in
- Settings › Models & accounts and first run (`providers/ProviderLogin.tsx`) run stock `login` on the tab's omp. While a sign-in is in flight its `open_url`, `input` and info `notify` requests (`isSignInRequest` in `shared/rpc-types.ts`) belong to the panel (`loginRequestIds` in `stores/extension-ui.ts`) and **never render as chat cards**, even after the panel unmounts, the page reloads or a paired host's link returns.
- Main counts `login` commands in flight per tab (`SidecarPool.noteSignIn`); for a paired host's tab also the host's `signIn` tab event (sent as `login` starts/ends, on every `tab.open` incl. a new tab's `{active: []}`, and ahead of `openAsks` on re-attach), because the host's login runs on when this window's `login` call fails with the link. A host status (not the link) leaving `ready` drops the host's list. Main marks each such request (`IpcSignIn`: `active`, provider, browser page) on the live `EXTENSION_UI` frame, in `EXTENSION_UI_OPEN` and in a host's re-sent `openAsks`; all reach the page through `pushTabExtensionUiRequest`: a marked request is never a card.
- While `login` runs (`active`) the page adopts the sign-in (`adoptedLogin`, counted in flight) and the panel shows it when reopened ("Sign-in in progress…", browser link, paste fallback, Cancel) until main sends `SIGN_IN_ENDED` (`endTabSignIn`: answers the leftover; panel reloads providers or shows the failure; outcome is unseen if a host's login ended while the link was down). Once `login` answered, a request is a leftover and is answered `cancelled` at once.
- Browser sign-in finishes through stock's loopback callback; stock also races a code paste and never closes it, so the panel shows the paste as a collapsed fallback (open on a paired host's tab) and answers the leftover `cancelled` once `login` returns (an empty answer earlier only re-asks).
- Stock's RPC `login` has no cancel and holds omp's serial command queue (up to its 5-min callback timeout), so Cancel sends `login_cancel` (`src/main/local/settings.ts`), ending it via the callback's own denial (`error=access_denied` with the flow's `state`). A relaunched client has lost a host login's browser page (Cancel of its adopted panel then answers the paste).

### Featured providers
- One picker for both sign-in surfaces: first run's "which provider do you use" step and Settings › Models & accounts › Add a provider (`providers/ProviderPicker.tsx`; list `FEATURED_PROVIDERS` in `providers/provider-catalog.ts`; `STARTER_PROVIDERS` holds the featured ids too so cards show before omp answers).
- Popular ones lead as their own group ("Popular providers"/"주요 프로바이더"; cards `[data-featured-provider]`: `ProviderMark` + name + one line + action, `min-h-11` = 44 px, `auto-fill minmax(15rem)` so a phone gets one column); the rest stay under "Other providers"/"다른 프로바이더". A search is the flat list of every match, no group.
- Ids and login kinds are stock's (pi-catalog `auth/*.kdl`, `/login` roster), never invented: ChatGPT (Plus/Pro) = `openai-codex` OAuth; Claude (Pro/Max) = `anthropic` OAuth; OpenRouter = `openrouter` (browser sign-in or paste yields a durable API key); Google (Gemini) = `google-gemini-cli` (stock's plain `google` has no login); Grok (SuperGrok) = `xai-oauth` (device-code, primary) with `xai` API key as the card's quieter second action (`apiKeyId`; subscription first; the key action is a quiet row INSIDE the card under a hairline, never a loose row under the grid).
- Card names are fixed brand labels; a card whose logins stock's catalog doesn't list is skipped. First run (`showConnectedFeatured`) shows a connected featured provider as "Connected" in place; Settings lists connected ones above and leaves them out.
- Tests: `ProviderPicker.test.tsx` (order, actions, connected, search), `e2e/first-run-no-omp.e2e.ts` (fake omp `OMP_GUI_TEST_FEATURED=1`, en/ko × light/dark), `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` (stock's real catalog lists all five in order), `e2e/mobile-pages.e2e.ts` (`?firstrun=1`: one column, 44 px, no overflow).

### Held stock defaults
- Sessions OMPdesk spawns (local tabs and tabs a host runs) differ from stock in `GUI_THINKING_DEFAULTS` (`resources/gui-bridge/thinking-default-core.ts`, wired by `thinking-default.ts`) unless some layer (config.yml, project config, `--config` overlay, env) sets them.
- Each is a runtime override applied inside the extension factory (stock loads `-e` extensions after `Settings.init`, before createAgentSession builds tools/advisor and picks the first thinking level). **Never written to config.yml** (the omp CLI keeps stock); shown in Settings with `runtime` provenance; released by a Settings write (user's value persists and wins); taken up again by a reset; inherited by subagents via stock's `createSubagentSettings` snapshot.
- Tests: `src/main/bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts` and `bridge-goal-continuation-stock.test.ts` (real omp); rules in `bridge-thinking-default.test.ts`.
- The list:
  - `defaultThinkingLevel` = `auto` (stock `high`), `providers.autoThinkingMaxEffort` = `max` (stock `xhigh`). The advisor role is written `:auto` when picked without a level (`resources/gui-bridge/role-values.ts`), because stock runs an unsuffixed advisor at fixed `medium`; pickers hide the suffix.
  - `goal.continuationModes` = `["interactive", "rpc"]` (stock continues a goal over RPC only with `"rpc"` listed); reached by id through `config/registry` `lookup` (omp's bundle has no `goals/settings`; see Bridge, Only the running omp's modules).
  - `display.showTokenUsage`, `lsp.diagnosticsOnEdit`, `lsp.formatOnWrite` on (the GUI usage row follows the first unless a GUI display pref overrides).
  - `astGrep.enabled` on (`astEdit.enabled` already is in stock). Both are the `@oh-my-pi/pi-natives` addon; `applyGuiThinkingDefault` feature-detects it (`NATIVE_DEFAULTS`) and an omp without it keeps stock's value with a Settings note (`SettingEntry.note`, `settings.note.native`).
  - `computer.enabled` on (stock off): the `computer` eval prelude (screenshots, input, accessibility of the host desktop; `/computer on|off|status` toggles per session; a Settings write releases the default). An omp without the setting is skipped (`lookup` finds no handle).
    - **Cost/risk**: the prelude adds prompt and tool text to every session and rides on `eval`; **Autopilot removes `eval` (`EXCLUDED_TOOLS` in `modes/autopilot.ts`), so computer use stays off there by design.**
    - Stock's `computerApproval`: `capabilities`/`read_only` need `read` approval, every input action `exec`. Stock's `tools.approvalMode` default is `yolo` and OMPdesk keeps it, so **by default a session can click and type on the user's desktop with no approval card**; only `always-ask` (or a rule) puts a card before input.
    - The prelude runs in the omp process, so on macOS Screen Recording and Accessibility go to the app that launched it (OMPdesk, or the terminal for a CLI omp). Without them stock fails the call ("macOS Screen Recording permission is not granted for this process" / "macOS Accessibility permission is required for native input") and `computer.capabilities()` reports `capturePermission`/`inputPermission`/`axPermission`. macOS never prompts from stock's preflight and OMPdesk adds no hint. Windows is stock's and untested by OMPdesk.
    - Pinned by `bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts` (resolves `true` as `runtime`, `eval` enabled, prelude active = `/computer status` "enabled · prelude: active") and opt-in `e2e/real-computer-use.e2e.ts` (read-only turn shows no card; an input turn under `always-ask` shows a card and is denied; nothing is ever approved).
  - `advisor.enabled` on: a second model (`advisor` role; unset follows `slow`, then stock's slow chain) reviews every turn, **one extra model call per turn**. Stock soft-pins it off for RPC hosts (`protocolDefault: ["rpc", "acp"]`, `applyProtocolDefaults` in `main.ts`); `PROTOCOL_PINNED` lets the override replace such a pin only while nothing but the pin sets the value (provenance `runtime`, value equals schema default, no persisted layer). Other advisor tuning (`advisor.syncBacklog`, …) stays stock's.
  - `title.icons` = `boring` (stock `nf+emoji`): plain-text titles, no icon/code card, no extra title-model request (`renameTitle` in stock `agent-session.ts`). Only new titles change; a stored carded title stays until ✨ or evolving makes a fresh one.
  - **OTLP telemetry export off** (the user's decision; not a setting). `telemetry.otlpExportEnabled` is read by `initTelemetryExport` in `main.ts` before extensions load, so no override reaches it. Instead every omp OMPdesk runs gets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` (`OMP_TELEMETRY_OFF` in `omp-runtime.ts`, spread last over the user's env: local/hosted tabs via `SidecarManager`, scheduled runs, benchmarks, model setup, `omp` CLI calls via `runtime.run`), on which stock's only exporter (`telemetry-export.ts`) returns at once whatever `OTEL_*` the user sets. Stats server and stock helper never call it; `omp --version`/`update` exit before it. Pinned by `shell-env.test.ts`.
  - **Not held** (stock 18.11.1): `display.showTurnTime`, `read.toolResultPreview` (TUI renderer only, `modes/utils/ui-helpers.ts`; the GUI hides `display.showTurnTime`); `retry.usageAwareFallback`, `retry.waitForUsageReset` (account rotation and interrupted-turn resume cover them).
  - **Automatic session titles on** (not a stock setting: stock's `autoTitle` is a session option `main.ts` sets for the TUI only, and `main.ts` sets `PI_NO_TITLE` for `--mode rpc-ui`, so GUI sessions stayed "New session"). Prefs `autoTitle` + sub-switch `autoTitleEvolve`, both default on (Settings › Agent behavior › Session titles). Not a runtime override: the bridge (`modes/auto-title.ts`, `modes/auto-title-engine.ts`) runs stock's title machinery from live session events, so `PI_NO_TITLE` can't reach it. The user's own `PI_NO_TITLE` is respected: main reads it from the env it hands omp (`titleSuppressedByEnv`) and passes `suppressed` in `OMP_GUI_AUTO_TITLE`/`auto_title_set`. Pinned by `bridge-auto-title*.test.ts`, `shell-env.test.ts`.

### Model roles
- Settings › Models & accounts › Model roles (`ModelRolesSection.tsx`, `ModelRoleRow.tsx`; contracts in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/model-role-contracts.ts`). Stock owns the roles (ids, names, `accepts`, empty-role fallbacks, `@role` resolution in `config/model-roles.ts`, `config/model-resolver.ts`); OMPdesk only groups them (`ROLE_GROUPS`): Conversation (default, slow, smol, plan), Agent work (task, advisor, commit, tiny), Input & output (vision, image, web, speech, dictation, judge), Custom roles. Stock's `memory` role is in `HIDDEN_ROLE_IDS` (it served only stock's memory backends, replaced by ai-memory).
- Per role: a localized one-line "when it is used" (`modelRoles.use.<id>`, grounded in the stock call site, e.g. `commit/model-selection.ts`, `utils/image-question.ts`, `auto-thinking/classifier.ts`) and the empty-role fallback text (`modelRoles.fallback.<id>`).
- `get_model_roles` adds `effective` per role (`role-effective.ts`: the model it really runs on via stock `resolveModelOverride` / `resolveRoleSelection` over the role's `roleCandidatePool`; `source` configured/fallback/session/none, `via` the answering role). Selector lists copy stock call sites, pinned by `src/main/bridge-model-roles-stock.test.ts` against `resolveRoleChain` and `resolveAdvisorRoleSelection` on the real omp.
- A row also shows the provider's account state and tightest live quota (`useUsageReports` in `hooks/use-usage-reports.ts`, the tab's one usage poll; `inUseReport`, `tightestLimit` in `lib/usage-reports.ts`), the thinking level the resolved selector names (else "follows session"), and a picker over stock's candidate pool.
- The model setup assistant proposes per group but assigns only stock's built-in roles; a custom role keeps the user's model.

### Custom roles
- `modelRoles.<id>`, selected as `@id`. Stock treats `@id` as a role only while `modelRoles.<id>` holds a value; otherwise it stays in an agent's `model:` list as a pattern matching nothing and resolution moves on (`getModelRoleAlias`, `resolveConfiguredRolePattern`, `resolveModelOverride`). So an agent lists the role first, then the built-ins it follows while empty (`["@review", "@slow", "@task"]`), and needs nothing assigned.
- Stock consumers: an agent definition's `model:` (`resolveAgentModelSelection` in `task/executor.ts`, `structured-subagent.ts`), `task.agentModelOverrides`, `/model` and `--model` (`resolveCliModel`, configured roles only), `cycleOrder` (a role needs a value to join).
- Six preset roles in `PRESET_ROLES` (explore, review, debug, docs, test, design); **never a role nothing consumes**: `AGENT_TEMPLATES` (`src/shared/agent-templates.ts`) carry `role` and a `model` of `roleSelectors(role)` (code-explorer, code-reviewer, debugger, docs-writer, test-writer, ui-polisher); `agent-templates.test.ts` fails on a preset without a template or a template naming a role outside the preset.
- The bridge lists presets while empty. Users add, rename, remove their own (`define_model_role` / `forget_model_role` → `model_role_define` / `model_role_forget`: `modelTags` with OMPdesk's `description`, `modelRoles`, `cycleOrder`). Rename changes only the display name (stock `modelTags.<id>`); **the id never changes** because agents select it. Each custom row lists agents using it (`get_agent_definitions` → `modelRoleRefs`) and template agents not installed yet.
- `e2e/model-roles-real.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_ROLES_REAL=1`, private copy of the user's config via `e2e/real-omp.ts`, no model call) resolves every role and template agent's model through stock; `OMP_GUI_ROLES_CAPTURES=<dir>` adds light/dark × en/ko, wide/narrow captures.

### Idle compaction and recap
- **Idle compaction**: stock's `compaction.idleEnabled` / `idleThresholdTokens` / `idleTimeoutSeconds` (Settings › Agent behavior › Context, `/autoclear on [delay] | off`) had a timer only in the TUI, so `resources/gui-bridge/idle-compaction.ts` (rules `idle-compaction-core.ts`, a copy of `#scheduleIdleCompaction`) arms it after a terminal `agent_end` and calls stock `runIdleCompaction()`. It and the idle recap share one idle-window state machine (`timer-core.ts` `IdleWindowTimer`: window, superseded arms, GUI draft); each keeps its own settings, delay bounds and fire. The renderer (`hooks/use-idle-compaction-draft.ts`) reports each tab's draft empty ⇄ non-empty via `set_idle_compaction_draft`, because stock skips a session whose input holds text.
- **Idle recap**: stock's `recap.enabled` (default on) / `recap.idleSeconds` (240) had a TUI-only timer (`#scheduleIdleRecap`), so `resources/gui-bridge/session-recap.ts` (rules `session-recap-core.ts`) arms it after a terminal `agent_end` and recaps as `#runIdleRecap` does: stock's `prompts/system/recap-user.md` with the goal (or title) and next todo, one `runEphemeralTurn`, `previewLine(…, TRUNCATE_LENGTHS.RECAP)`, journaled with `sessionManager.recordRecap`.
  - Skipped while the composer holds a draft (same `set_idle_compaction_draft` report, also forwarded by `local/settings.ts` as `recap_draft`); cancelled by any new turn, compaction or typing; re-checked for idleness after the reply; follows `recap.*` writes (`recapSettingChanged`).
  - Delivery: stock's TUI notice is transient, so the GUI gets a displayed `recap` custom message (`custom_message` journal entry the transcript replays; `RecapCard` in `CustomMessageCard.tsx`, one quiet muted line, `chat.custom.recap`) and a `context` hook drops it from every model request.
  - Tests: `src/main/bridge-session-recap-stock.test.ts` (real omp), `bridge-session-recap.test.ts`; fake omp serves `recap_draft` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_RECAP` (`e2e/omp-defaults.e2e.ts`); `sessionRecap` in `capabilities.ts` / `health.ts`.

### Context inspector, prompt sections, prompt origins
- **Context inspector** (Insights › This session › Inspect, `components/insights/ContextInspector.tsx`): bridge `context.inspect` (`resources/gui-bridge/context-inspect.ts`, rules `context-inspect-core.ts`) keeps stock `getContextBreakdown()`'s anchored totals, splits tool schemas by source (built-in / MCP / extension, per tool via stock `estimateToolSchemaTokens`) and reads context files back out of the rendered `<repo-rules>` block. `context.entry` returns one entry's provider text (tool schema via `toolWireSchema`, messages via `convertToLlm`), capped at 32k characters.
- **Per-turn prompt sections**: stock drops what `before_agent_start` appended when a turn ends (and a base-prompt rebuild replaces it), so `session.systemPrompt` lacks OMPdesk's sections (reply language, profile, reply style, visuals) before the first turn and after a rebuild. Each mode registers its section via `resources/gui-bridge/prompt-sections.ts` (`registerPromptSection`: one "append if missing" step per id, hooked into `before_agent_start`); the inspector applies them in install order (`withTurnSections`) to preview the next prompt. **A new mode that appends to the system prompt registers there, never with a bare `pi.on("before_agent_start")`.**
- **Prompt origins** (`resources/gui-bridge/prompt-origin.ts`, contracts `prompt-origin-contracts.ts`): a host passes the device a tab runs for (and its own name) as `OMP_GUI_PROMPT_ORIGIN` at spawn; that omp journals one `ompdesk.prompt-origin` custom entry per user message (public `message_end` hook, keyed by message timestamp); `sessions.transcript` sets `promptFrom` on prompts typed on another device than the viewing one once a session holds prompts from two devices (MessageBubble: "from {device}"). Each device's tab is its own omp; the one other party that types into it or answers its asks is a window of the host itself (a hosted view, see Remote devices): its prompts are journaled as the host's (`prompt_origin_set` with `PROMPT_ORIGIN_HOST_ID`), and the first answer to an ask, the device's or the host window's, wins.

## Machine setup and updates

### Machine setup (`src/main/setup/`, contract `src/shared/setup.ts`)
- At launch main detects Bun, omp (through `resolveOmp`, so `MIN_OMP_VERSION` gates it), Git, GitHub CLI, Node.js (through the REA probe) and Tailscale, and installs what is missing **on its own**: no install button, no consent click. `SETUP_ITEMS` = bun, git, omp, gh, node, tailscale.
  - Required, in order: Bun (`curl -fsSL https://bun.sh/install | bash` / `irm bun.sh/install.ps1 | iex`, user-level); on Windows Git for Windows (an existing install with `bin\bash.exe` is reused, else the PortableGit pinned in `plans.ts` with its SHA-256, extracted without admin into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ompdesk\tools\git`; never WSL); then omp (`<abs bun> install -g @oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent`, or `omp update` below the minimum).
  - Optional, in the background once omp works: Git on macOS (`xcode-select --install`); gh and Node (Homebrew on macOS, else the official archive checked against its published SHA-256 into the managed tools dir `<userData>/tools`; `winget … --accept-source-agreements --accept-package-agreements` on Windows, else `unavailable`).
  - Tailscale (Mobile web) is never automatic (`setupItemAutomatic`): only `installForConnection` and an explicit Retry install it; the user's click is its consent.
  - Claude Code CLI and Codex CLI are never installed: omp signs in to Anthropic and OpenAI itself.
- `download.ts` fetches PortableGit and archives itself (streamed to `<tools>/.downloads`, SHA-256 checked, removed after the install) so an item's `progress` carries real bytes; running items also carry `step`/`steps` and `startedAt`. The view shows a determinate bar only for bytes, else an indeterminate one, plus "Step n of m" and the elapsed time.
- `paths.ts` appends the managed bin dirs to `process.env.PATH` and sets `GIT_INSTALL_ROOT` (stock `resolveWindowsShell` checks it after the Git for Windows roots) at startup and after each install. After an install it re-reads the login-shell PATH (macOS) or registry PATH (Windows), re-detects, and when omp became usable restarts every local tab in `error`/`exited` and the stats server. No app restart.
- Retry policy: each item once per launch automatically; after three automatic misses only once a day (`BACKOFF_MS` 24 h, `<userData>/setup-state.json`); the user's Retry always runs.
- Every install runs under `supervisor.ts` (the app binary as Node via `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`), which kills the installer's process tree when its stdin closes: Cancel (also aborts main's download), quit (`SetupService.dispose`) or a crash.
- IPC (`setup:get-state`, `setup:retry`, `setup:cancel`, push `setup:state`) takes item ids only; no handler accepts a command.
- Automatic installs run only in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`, or with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` (plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_DOWNLOAD`/`OMP_GUI_TEST_DOWNLOAD_SHA256`: a file every test plan downloads first). Dev and test launches otherwise only detect.
- Setup screen (`components/onboarding/SetupTakeover.tsx`, shown by `App.tsx` from `useMachineSetupNeeded()`): while a local tab's omp is unusable and a required item is missing or installing, one modal dialog covers the whole window (sidebar and title bar included; drag strip and OS window controls stay live). Everything beneath is `inert`; menu actions (except the language switch; the tray's "new session" included), omp:// links, shortcuts and drops are no-ops through `useMachineSetupStore.takeover`; Tab is trapped. When required setup finishes it fades out (reduced motion skips the fade) into the first-run flow (`FirstRunGate` → provider step, "Install omp" shown done). Optional items and remote tabs never show it. Every item stays listed in Settings › Updates & about › This computer (`MachineSetupSection.tsx`).
- `.github/workflows/setup-assets-health.yml` downloads every pinned setup file weekly through `download.ts` (`scripts/setup-assets-health.ts`; `gh workflow run setup-assets-health.yml` runs it now) and keeps one issue open while any fails.

### Connect installs its tool
- `SetupService.installForConnection`: a connection the user starts that needs a missing optional item installs it right then, as the user's own action: past the daily back-off and the once-per-launch rule, queued behind any running install, only in a launch that installs on its own (else it only detects). The connection carries on when the tool lands. Callers: gh (GitHub sign-in), node (`LocalMachine.setBuiltinRea` turning the built-in REA on while Node is missing or too old), tailscale (`tailscale/service.ts`).
- Connect GitHub (Settings › General, the first run's GitHub step, the PR center's signed-out card; `onboarding/GithubSignIn.tsx`) calls `GithubAuthService.startLogin`: with gh missing the flow is `installing`, awaits `installGh`, then starts `gh auth login`. No gh after the install → `gh_install_failed`; nothing able to install it here → `gh_missing`; Cancel stops it (`cancelGhInstall`).
- `onboarding/SetupInstallInline.tsx` shows the item inline (step, bytes, clock; the reason plus Retry after a failure; the download page when nothing can install it), also for setup's own background gh install. A paired host's tab: the host's own UI and setup install on the host.
- git/gh lookups (`local/git/exec.ts` `resolveBin`, `execBin`) read the spawn PATH on every call (`spawnPath` shares the cached login-shell probe, which `refreshPath` drops after an install), so a fresh gh is found without a relaunch. Never keep a copy of PATH from the first git call.
- No stock omp provider login shells out to a CLI (github-copilot, gemini-cli, google-antigravity, cursor, anthropic, openai-codex are OAuth/device-code flows in pi-ai's `registry/oauth/`; perplexity only reads its macOS app's token), so no provider sign-in installs anything.
- `e2e/github-cli-install.e2e.ts` (fake installer) covers gh off PATH and in back-off, the click downloading (held at half), failing offline with Retry, installing into the managed tools dir and reaching the device code and signed-in account (en/ko captures).

### omp updates itself (`src/main/omp-updater.ts`, contract `src/shared/omp-update.ts`)
- OMPdesk's own update is `updater.ts`, installed by hand unless the opt-in `appAutoUpdate` (default off) is on.
- Main asks stock `omp update --check` (`SetupService.latestOmp`) at launch (after machine setup's first pass) and every 4 h, doubling the wait per failure in a row up to 24 h; while omp is missing nothing is checked. When the answer is newer than the installed omp (`compareVersions`) it runs stock `omp update` through `SetupService.updateOmp` (the omp item's `omp-update` plan under the setup supervisor): background, no click, queued behind any install, **never waiting for a turn to end** (the user's rule: a running omp keeps the code it loaded).
- Check and update share one environment (`ompUpdateEnv` in `omp-runtime.ts`): the detected omp's folder first on PATH (stock updates the first `omp` on PATH, `resolveOmpPath` = `$which("omp")`) and, for a `bun install -g` launcher, `BUN_INSTALL=<its Bun root>` (stock calls it a Bun install only inside `bun pm bin -g`). So both follow the user's registry (.npmrc, bunfig, `npm_config_registry`), channel and install method (Bun, npm, Homebrew, mise, standalone), agree, and land in the omp OMPdesk runs, never another `omp` earlier on PATH. `get_omp_update` (a paired host's check, `local/misc.ts`) uses it too.
- Why it is safe: `bun install -g` replaces package files by new inodes, and the bridge's literal `@oh-my-pi/*` imports resolve to the running omp's bundled singletons (`health.ts`). Processes that read omp's files from disk (stats server, stock helper: `import(variable)`) are stopped before the installer and started after it. Known limit: the old process's lazy hashed assets in `dist/` (HTML export template, highlight/marked chunks) vanish, so those can fail in a tab until it restarts. Windows locks files a running omp holds: stock's EBUSY/launcher repair applies; a failed install is kept in `OmpUpdateState.failure` and retried at the next check.
- After success main re-detects omp (`refreshSystemOmp`), starts the stats server on it, restarts every idle local tab and every idle tab it hosts for paired devices on their own session file, and restarts a busy tab one tick after its turn ends (`src/main/omp-restart.ts`; `SidecarPool.restartOnNewOmp`, `LocalHostServices.restartOnNewOmp`; `asleep` tabs need nothing). Composer drafts survive. The restarts re-ask each bridge's `capabilities` (`BridgeHealth` is keyed by omp version), so a feature the new omp lacks shows the existing `unsupported_omp` warning toast. Success shows one quiet "omp updated to X" toast in one window.
- Pref `ompAutoUpdate` (default on; a stored `false` turns it off; `OMP_AUTO_UPDATE_PREF`) and the manual "Update now" live in Settings › Updates & about › This computer (`OmpUpdateRows.tsx`). IPC (`omp-update:get-state`, `omp-update:now`, push `omp-update:state`, `omp-update:updated`) carries no version, command or path.
- Each computer updates its own omp under its own pref, always through its own `OmpUpdater`. On a paired host's tab Settings › Updates & about shows the host's row (`HostOmpUpdateRow`); its button only asks the host to run its updater (`host.ompUpdate.now` / `host-omp-update:now`; state `host.ompUpdate.get` / `host-omp-update:get`; the host pushes every change as the `host.ompUpdate.changed` event, forwarded as `host-omp-update:state`), so the host's helpers, supervisor, re-detection and tab restarts run, and it never overlaps its own scheduled update. There is no `omp_update` RPC and no second `omp update` path.
- Scheduled checks run only in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`, or in a test launch with both `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_OMP_UPDATES=1` (installer called `<installer> omp omp-update`).
- Tests: `e2e/omp-auto-update.e2e.ts` with `OMP_GUI_TEST_LATEST_OMP` (what `update --check` reports), `OMP_GUI_TEST_UPDATE_CHECK_LOG` (each check's first PATH entry), `OMP_GUI_TEST_VERSION_FILE`, `OMP_GUI_TEST_BOOT_LOG`, `OMP_GUI_TEST_UNAVAILABLE_AFTER_UPDATE`; `src/main/setup/omp-update-install.test.ts` runs the real `bun install -g` against a fake registry (`src/main/test-omp-registry.ts`) in a temp HOME and BUN_INSTALL, a second omp first on PATH staying untouched.

### `UpdateScheduler` (`src/main/update-scheduler.ts`)
- The omp, tool CLI and extension updaters share one loop: it ticks at start in a scheduled launch, re-arms its timer from the end of each tick (a rejected tick does not end the schedule), queues passes one at a time (`enqueue`, dropped once disposed) and owns the back-off (`nextCheckDelay`, per item `settleBackoff`; `CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` 4 h, `MAX_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` 24 h).
- omp backs off its timer by its own failure count and joins a running check instead of queueing a second; CLI and extension updaters back off per tool or row (a cancel is no failure). `updater.ts` (its own 5-minute check) is not on it.

### Tool CLIs of connected accounts (`src/main/cli-tools/`, contract `src/shared/cli-update.ts`)
- Pref `cliAutoUpdate` (default on), its own switch in Settings › Updates & about › This computer (a user may pin a vendor CLI; OMPdesk needs no minimum version of one). Runs at launch (after setup's first pass) and every 4 h with omp's per-tool back-off (`CliUpdater`).
- Main reads providers with a stored credential from stock's own store (stock-helper `accounts.providers` = `discoverAuthStorage().credentials.list()`, ids only; `sdk` is listed under `providers` in `capabilities.ts`) and maps them to CLIs (`catalog.ts`: anthropic → `claude`, openai-codex/openai → `codex`, google-gemini-cli/google → `gemini`, cursor → `cursor-agent`, github-copilot → `copilot`, `gh` and its `gh-copilot` extension), plus what setup installs (gh, and Node in the managed tools dir only).
- It **never installs** a vendor CLI: only an installed one is listed and updated, by the owner found from its resolved executable path (`detect.ts classifyInstall`): Homebrew (`brew upgrade [--cask] <name>`), a global npm (`npm install -g --prefix <that prefix>`) or Bun package (`bun install -g` under that `BUN_INSTALL`), winget (`winget upgrade --id`), the vendor's updater (`claude update`, which also owns a Claude Code in npm/Bun; `cursor-agent update`; `gh extension upgrade gh-copilot`), or setup's verified archive for the managed gh/Node. System (`/usr/bin`, Xcode CLT, `C:\Windows`), app-bundled (the Codex in ChatGPT.app) and admin-only installs are listed with their reason and never touched; no sudo.
- The check asks the owner first (`npm view`, `bun pm view`, `brew outdated --json=v2`, `winget list --upgrade-available`, the release feed), updates only when newer (`compareVersions`), runs `SetupService.runToolUpdate` under the setup supervisor (queued behind installs, never touching setup items' state) and detects again (`updated` or `not-updated`).
- Running-process rule: npm and Bun replace the whole package dir (a running process keeps its open files, but a later `require` reads the new version and a file only the old version had is gone); Homebrew removes the old keg. So on macOS an npm/Bun/Homebrew update of a Node CLI waits while a process of it runs (`ps`, `plans.ts waitsForIdle`/`runningIn`; result `deferred`, no back-off, retried next check). A native binary (gh), vendor updaters and managed copies update at once. On Windows a file a running tool holds just fails the update, which is recorded and retried. A failure backs off like omp's.
- IPC `cliUpdate.getState/now(id?)/onState` carries tool ids only (an unknown id throws; no command, path or version crosses it); opening Settings rescans. Each computer, and each paired host, updates its own tools.
- Homebrew prefixes (`/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`) are also searched when the login-shell PATH omits them (`resolveBin` for gh/git, setup's gh detection, `cli-tools/detect.ts`). The PR and GitHub centers show the connect card (install, then sign in) instead of a download link for a missing gh on this computer; a paired host's tab keeps the link (that gh is the host's).
- Tests: a test launch checks on its own only with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_CLI_UPDATES=1` (runs `<installer> cli-<tool> <method> check|update`; `e2e/cli-update.e2e.ts`). `OMP_GUI_TEST_BREW_DIRS` (PATH-style list, empty = none) replaces Homebrew's prefixes so the developer's brew never answers a test.
- Windows: a tool is found only as `<bin>.exe` or `<bin>.cmd` (npm's bare `<bin>` and `npm` are POSIX scripts Windows cannot start). A `.cmd` runs through cmd.exe with the omp shim's quoting (`src/main/spawn.ts`: `launcherNames`, `findOnPath`, `spawnCommand`/`spawnArgs`/`needsShell`: the one PATH lookup and Windows spawn rule every external tool uses). npm writes a global bin there as a cmd-shim, so `detect.ts cmdShimTarget` reads the package file the shim runs (`"%dp0%\node_modules\<package>\<bin>"`) to find its npm prefix. A Bun global's `.exe` + `.bunx` shim is not read and lists as `unknown-install`.

### Extensions of connected omp (`src/main/ext-update/`, contract `src/shared/extension-update.ts`, helper contract `resources/gui-bridge/stock-helper/extensions-contracts.ts`)
- Pref `extensionAutoUpdate` (default on), its own switch in Settings › Updates & about › This computer › Extensions. Marketplaces, plugins, skills and MCP servers, on `CliUpdater`'s schedule (after setup's first pass, every 4 h, per-row back-off `nextCheckDelay`, failures recorded, deferred rows retried; `omp` being installed defers without back-off). `ExtensionUpdater` reads the inventory in the extensions worker (local files, no network) and works marketplaces → plugins → skills → MCP, one row at a time.
- Marketplaces: stock `updateMarketplace` (what `marketplace_action update` runs).
- Plugins: marketplace installs by stock `checkForUpdates`/`upgradePlugin`, npm and git plugins by `PluginManager.upgrade`. Stock has no user pin for a marketplace plugin (the catalog's `ref`/`sha`/`version` is the author's; upgrade follows it).
  - `upgrade(name)` reinstalls an npm plugin at the registry's latest whatever its spec says, so OMPdesk reads `plugins/package.json` and calls upgrade only when the spec is what a bare install writes (bun 1.4.2: `pkg`/`pkg@latest` → `^x.y.z`, `pkg@beta` → `^<tagged>`, exact and range specs verbatim; `^<installed>`, `latest`, `*` follow latest) and latest is strictly newer.
  - An exact or ranged npm spec and a git commit/version-tag ref are pinned: checked, shown as "update available", never moved, not even by Update now. Linked plugins are skipped.
  - npm/git/linked plugins are user scope only (stock's `PluginManager` has no project scope); marketplace plugins also per project registry of the open local tabs' folders.
- Skills: stock records a source only for skills-registry installs (`skills.json`/`skills.lock.json`, `skillshare/manifest.ts`; `omp skill install|update|uninstall` take `@scope/name`; no git-source skill), so those update by `updateSkillPackages` (exact range = pinned; a version that ships scripts is never approved for the user: `needs-approval`). Plugin-provided skills follow their plugin (one grouped row); plain local files are one "local, not updated" row.
- MCP servers (the user's config is never written):
  - The built-in REA is listed as pinned to the OMPdesk release; URL servers are skipped (`remote`).
  - An unpinned `npx`/`bunx`/`uvx` package is "follows latest at start" and its cache is not touched (npm 11.17: a bare `npx -y pkg` after a new publish starts the new version; warming a cache would execute the server). A pinned `pkg@1.2.3` is checked against the registry and shown as an available update, config untouched.
  - A global binary is updated by its owner (`cli-tools/detect.ts classifyInstall` plus pipx `/pipx/venvs/<pkg>/`, uv tool `/uv/tools/<pkg>/`, pip via the shebang python's `importlib.metadata`) through `SetupService.runToolUpdate` under the supervisor (system, bundled and admin-only installs listed and skipped, no sudo).
  - Running rule: a script (shebang) whose owner replaces package files in place (npm/Bun, Homebrew, pipx/uv/pip) waits while a `ps`-visible process of it runs (`deferred running`, retried); a native binary updates at once; Windows has no scan, a held file fails and is retried.
- Applying to live tabs (`src/main/extension-reload.ts`, `SidecarPool.applyExtensionUpdate`, `LocalHostServices.applyExtensionUpdate`): one `ExtensionApplyPlan` per pass. An idle ready tab reloads now (bridge `extension_update.reload` = stock `reloadPluginState`, what `/reload-plugins` does); a plugin that ships extensions/tools/hooks (`activation: restart-required`, `extensions/activation-core.ts`) restarts the tab on its session file (`omp-restart.ts` pattern); an updated MCP package is reconnected (`extension_update.reconnect` = stock `MCPManager.reconnectServer`); a busy tab waits and applies one tick after its turn ends; `asleep` tabs need nothing.
- Every stock internal the worker uses is in `capabilities.ts` and `health.ts` `LOADERS`. IPC `extensionUpdate.getState/now(key?)/onState` carries row keys only. Each computer, and each paired host, updates its own.
- Tests: a test launch checks only with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_EXT_UPDATES=1` (MCP binary checks/updates run `<installer> cli-mcp-<name> <method> check|update`; `e2e/extension-update.e2e.ts`).

### Scheduled runs (Settings › Schedules; contract `src/shared/schedules.ts`, service `src/main/schedules.ts`)
- Main checks every 30 s (`SCHEDULE_TICK_MS`) and starts stock `omp -p [--model] [--yolo]` in the schedule's folder, only while OMPdesk is open. Times missed while it was closed are skipped; a start due while the previous run still goes is recorded as skipped. Stock omp has no scheduler.
- Each run goes through the setup supervisor (`spawnSupervised`, the prompt file as omp's stdin), so a Stop, a quit or a crash kills omp's whole tree.
- Schedules and the last 200 runs (`SCHEDULE_RUNS_KEPT`) live in `<userData>/schedules.json`; run output in `<userData>/schedule-runs/<runId>.log`.

## Remote devices

### Pairing, transport, routing
- Code: `src/main/remote/`. OMPdesk on a host (B) runs tabs for paired clients (A).
- Pairing is an 8-char code shown on B (10 min, single use; 5 wrong guesses rotate it) proved through an X25519 + HKDF handshake. Later connections authenticate both long-term keys (Noise-KK-like) and run AES-256-GCM frames over TCP.
- Keys and peers live in `<userData>/remote-*.json` (`remote-identity.json`, `remote-peers.json`, `remote-hosts.json`; mode 0600, no safeStorage).
- The pool holds a `SidecarLike` per tab. A `RemoteSidecar` (A) proxies `tab.*` requests to B's `LocalHostServices`, which runs real SidecarManagers from `sidecar-factory.ts` and forwards their events.
- `ipc.ts` routes every handler that touches the omp machine (sessions, fs reads, launch profiles, project switches) by the tab's `device`. F-OWN keys are device + path.
- Settings, Usage and Stats follow the focused tab's `device`. Tab RPC already reaches the host. models.yml providers, the stats server and `omp bench` go through `machine-services.ts` (`LocalMachine` here, `host.*` calls on B). App prefs, the updater and remote-devices stay this device's (`SECTION_SCOPE`, `components/remote/HostScope.tsx`).
- Host tool calls (`gui_open_url`, notify, clipboard) always run on A.
- `OMP_GUI_REMOTE_PORT` (TCP, default 47821, `0` = ephemeral) and `OMP_GUI_REMOTE_DISCOVERY_PORT` (UDP beacon, default 47822) let parallel instances coexist.

### Device names
- **One name per device, the one it announces** (the user's rule, 19 Oct 2026; never a per-computer alias). This computer's is pref `remote.deviceName` (default the OS hostname; the OS computer name is never changed). Settings › Devices & connections shows the field always, sharing on or off (it was hidden while sharing was off, so a device that only connects to others could not rename itself).
- Renamable at any time from either side (`RemoteDevices`): `setDeviceName` saves and announces live, to every connected device as the event `host.name` (`RemoteHost.announceName`) and to every host this computer is connected to as the request `peer.name` (served by `RemoteHost` itself, it updates `remote-peers.json`); offline ones learn it from the next hello (which already carried the name, so before this a rename reached others only on reconnect). The beacon reads the name live.
- A host row's Rename (`REMOTE_RENAME_HOST` → request `host.rename`, served by `RemoteHost`, logged on the host) makes the host rename itself; a paired-device row's Rename (`REMOTE_RENAME_PEER`) sends that device the event `device.rename` and it renames itself; refused `remote_offline` while it is not connected (the button is disabled with the reason). Names are 1–64 characters (`DeviceNameSchema` in `app-protocol.ts`).
- A phone-page device's name is this computer's record of it (`devices-store.ts` `rename`, `WEB_DEVICES_RENAME`); Tailscale's node name is only the default.
- Agents rename through `ompdesk_app` `remote.devices` (read), `remote.rename_this_computer`, `remote.rename_host`, `remote.rename_device`, `mobile_web.rename_device` (change); `ompdesk_sessions` `device` resolves by id or current name.
- Inline field: `settings/DeviceNameField.tsx`, shared by the remote and phone-page rows. Pinned by `remote/devices-rename.test.ts` (two `RemoteDevices` over real sockets), `RemoteSection.test.tsx`, `e2e/device-rename.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_DEVICE_RENAME=<dir>` + `_THEME` captures in Korean).

### Per-device model limit
- A paired device's per-day model limit (`remote/peer-quota.ts`, prefs on the peer in `remote-peers.json`) covers every RPC command that calls a model on the host, plus `host.bench.run` (models × runs requests) and `host.modelSetup.run`.
- `remote/quota-commands.ts` classifies **every** `RpcCommand` type as model, conditional (`switch_leaf` with `summarize`; `plan_approval` that runs or sends feedback; `set_goal` with an objective or `resume`) or free. A compile-time check and `quota-commands.test.ts` fail when a new command type is in none of the lists, and a type no list knows counts.
- `LocalHostServices` reserves the request (`PeerQuota.reserve`, no await between the check and the count; a weighted reservation that would take the day past its limit is refused whole) and refunds it when the work throws or fails.
- Terminals (`terminal.request`) and the agent's `bash`/`eval` stay outside the limit.

### Link loss: a tab never loses the user's answer
- `RemoteSidecar.sendSideChannel` throws `remote_offline` for an `extension_ui_response` while the link is down. Otherwise it returns its delivery (sent once a pending `tab.open` re-attach finished), which rejects with `remote_offline` when the link drops under it or with the host's error when the host refuses it.
- `routeSideChannel` and `EXTENSION_UI_RESPOND` await it, so the pool keeps the request route, the IPC call rejects, and the card or sign-in prompt stays open with an error toast.
- Replies to the host's tool/URI calls are queued and sent once the host tab re-attaches. A `tab.close` that could not be sent goes to `PendingTabCloses` (kept per host link in `devices.ts`, sent when the link is back).
- A remote tab's own link statuses (`error` on link loss, `starting` on reconnect; `statusFromLink`) never drop its open asks in the pool, so a reload still gets the card back. A status the host reports (its omp exited or restarted, or its refusal of the re-attach's `tab.open`/`tab.start` over a live link) does.
- Re-attach brings what changed while the link was down. Every `tab.open` of a tab the host still holds re-sends the asks its omp has open (the `openAsks` tab event; `LocalHostServices` keeps each ask's body until the device answers it or omp withdraws it). The pool replaces the tab's open set with that list:
  - a card whose ask is gone (omp restarted or withdrew it) closes through a synthesized `cancel`;
  - each open ask is forwarded again, since the reconnect's `starting` cleared the page (`pushTabExtensionUiRequest` never doubles a card still up);
  - an ask the tab already held goes out marked `resent` (`IpcExtensionUiPayload`), so the OS "needs you" notification is not repeated; an ask raised while the link was down notifies as new;
  - an ask whose answer is still on its way (`routeSideChannel`) is left as it is.
- `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` cuts the link through a relay (`linkProxy` in `remote-harness.ts`) to cover both: the card comes back, and after the host's omp restarted none stays, not even after a reload.

### Host windows see the device's tabs
- **The user's rule (19 Oct 2026): the person at the host sees and can work in every session running on that computer, including one a paired device started** (e.g. with `ompdesk_session_open {device}`).
- Why: a hosted tab lives in `LocalHostServices`, not in the host's pool, so the host's sidebar had no tab for its session, and an agent-opened session (its first prompt an `<ompdesk-agent-message>` brief whose opener is on the device) folded away as a finished helper (`foldedRootOf` → `familyChildFor` `finished` → `isShownOpened` false); opening it spawned or switched a second omp onto the file.
- List: `LocalHostServices.hostedSessions()` (`IpcHostedSession`: path, id, title, cwd, kind, status with `running`, `awaitingInput`, the device's name; `onHostedSessionsChange` fires only on a real change) is `HOSTED_SESSIONS`, pushed as `HOSTED_SESSIONS_CHANGED` to every window (renderer `stores/hosted-sessions.ts`, synced in App, re-read on a web page's resync). The sidebar counts each as held by a tab (never folded, never archived out, its project expanded), gives it a live row before the index lists it, and takes its signal (running, needs input) from that state.
- Open: opening it views the device's tab. `spawnTabForWindow` (dep `hosted`) asks `viewSession(path)` for a `HostedTabView` (`remote/hosted-view.ts`, a `SidecarLike` over the hosted sidecar) and `SidecarPool.acquireHostedView` makes it the window's tab (`PoolEntry.hostedView`: listed `shared`, so the host decides steer/follow-up; owns the session for F-OWN). It is never in `resumeFacts`, `tabInventory`, the saved layout, `workspaceDirs`, `restartOnNewOmp` or `applyExtensionUpdate`, which are the device tab's. `GET_SESSION_OWNER` answers `{hosted: true}` and `switch_session` refuses `session_owned_elsewhere {hosted: true}`, both routed by the renderer to a tab (`routeToSessionOwner`).
- The view forwards the omp's stream but never `hostToolCall`/`hostUriRequest` (the device answers those), journals a prompt typed here as the host's and relabels a transcript page for the host (`labelForHost`). An ask's first answer, the device's or the window's, wins (`#answer`: the other side gets a `cancel` `answered:<id>`, which the pool marks `answeredElsewhere`, and its later answer fails `already_answered`).
- Lifetime: closing the view only leaves (`CLOSE_TAB` releases the entry; the omp runs on); a viewed tab outlives the device's grace period; the device's close, delete, revoke or the grace's end ends every view first (`VIEW_ENDED_EVENT`: the pool releases the entry and the window's list drops the shared tab, no error tab). A host-side delete of a session a device's tab runs is refused (`session_running`).
- Tests: `host-services.test.ts` ("for this computer's own windows"), the hosted-view cases of `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `tab-spawn.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, and `e2e/remote-host-view.e2e.ts` (device opens a session on the host → the host lists it once in its project → opens it as a view with no second omp launched → a prompt from each side reaches the one omp and shows on both → the device closes its tab → the host's view ends with no error tab).

### Devices see the host windows' tabs
- **The other way round (the user's duplicate-tabs bug, 19 Oct 2026): a device opening a session a window of the host already has open never gets a second omp.**
- Attach: `LocalHostServices.#open` asks `localTabs.viewForDevice(path, {deviceId, name}, hostName)` first; `SidecarPool.viewForDevice` hands out a `HostedTabView` of that window's tab (`PoolEntry.deviceViews`; the same view class, its `promptOrigin` the device, its transcript localized for it with `localizePromptOrigins`). The host tab holds it as its sidecar (`HostTab.viewsWindow`: never in `resumeFacts`, `hostedSessions`, `restartOnNewOmp` or `applyExtensionUpdate`; the window's open asks reach the device as `openAsks`).
- Device side: `tab.open` answers `{attached: true, viewing: true}` (`TabOpenResult.viewing`), so the device starts nothing and its `RemoteSidecar` reports `shared` (event `shared` → the pool re-lists the tab as shared: host decides steer/follow-up, closing it only leaves).
- Asks: the window's tab counts the device in `viewerCount`; the first answer, the device's (`#answerFromDevice`) or a window's (`routeSideChannel`), wins with the same `answered:<id>` cancel and `already_answered` refusal.
- Lifetime: the device's close only leaves; the window's close ends every device view first (`#releaseEntry` → `VIEW_ENDED_EVENT` → tab event `viewEnded` → the device's pool releases its tab quietly). A delete while the other side holds the session is refused both ways: the host's (`deviceViewing`) and the device's (`sessions.delete` while a window here runs it live).
- Tests: the "device views" cases of `sidecar-pool.test.ts`, "a device opening a session a window of this computer runs" in `host-services.test.ts`, and the second half of `e2e/remote-host-view.e2e.ts` (host opens first → device opens it, no new omp launched → prompts from both reach the one omp and show on both → the host closes → the device's view ends, no error tab).

## Interrupted work, quit and diagnostics

### Interrupted turns continue by themselves
- `src/main/turn-resume.ts`, `src/main/connectivity.ts`. Settings › General › Continue interrupted work after relaunch, pref `resumeInterrupted`, **default on** (main reads `!== false`; the "default off" comments on `resumeInterrupted`, `scheduledRetry`, `batchNotifications` in `src/shared/desktop-prefs.ts` are stale).
- **Two paths, one coordinator (`TurnResume`), driven by `SidecarPool.onRunningChange` (`resumeFacts()`).**
- **① Relaunch.** Eligibility is a marker file `<userData>/interrupted-turns.json` ({window, tab, peer, sessionPath, cwd, kind, startedAt, reason}) rewritten atomically on every run-state transition. NEVER write it in a quit hook: SIGKILL/crash runs none.
  - Present while a turn runs (`running`); cleared on normal completion and on the user's Stop (`SidecarPool.noteStopped`, called by the RPC dispatcher for `STOP_COMMANDS` `abort`/`abort_and_prompt`/`abort_and_restore_queue`, so a kill before the run's end event leaves none); kept as `asking` while blocked on an ask/approval (the relaunch re-runs the cut-off call, the card reopens; not "continuing"). An omp that exits or restarts takes its open asks with it (the pool forgets owners on any non-ready status).
  - A quit (`quitApproved()`) freezes writes: tearing tabs down must not read as turns ending. Closing the last shown window on Windows with close-to-tray off is such a quit, asked for before the window goes (`lastWindowCloseAction`, `quit-guard.ts`).
  - A marker whose session the user deleted (`deletedSessions()` tombstone) is dropped at launch, never continued, not even into a fresh tab. A never-written session has no tombstone and still continues.
  - `TurnResume.launch()` (after saved windows exist): each marker finds its tab by session path (deferred tab: `SidecarPool.startTab`; missing from the layout: opened) and continues through stock: `restore_interrupted` (local handler → bridge `composer.restore_interrupted` → stock `restoreSession`, what `omp --restore` runs) for a cut-off turn, `retry` (`/retry`, the quota-resume path) for a failed one. A killed OMPdesk's omp usually closes the turn itself on stdin EOF (pending ask fails "RPC client disconnected before extension UI response completed", run journaled aborted), so `restoreSession` finds nothing; the relaunch then runs stock `retry`, which replays the failed tool batch or aborted reply and does nothing for a turn that really ended.
  - A continuation waits for its tab's omp (`RESUME_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`, 5 min; an omp that already failed drops the marker at once). The resuming row's Stop, the waiting row's Cancel and a new prompt on a held tab call it off even during the wait (a per-tab generation checked before every command).
  - `restore_interrupted` and `retry` get `RESUME_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` (2 min), not the RPC client's 8 s default: stock's restore reads every subagent transcript first (v0.37.0, 577 transcripts: reply cut, logged `failed`, stock kept reviving).
  - No toast: each resuming tab shows its own calm row ("Continuing where it left off…", `data-resuming`, one per tab, `setResuming` → tab `resuming` → `TurnStatusRow`; not for a reopened card) with the Stop on it (`resume.stop(tabId)` → `TurnResume.stopResumed(tabId)`: that tab only, noted as the user's Stop so its marker goes and Autopilot's goal resume ends `cancelled`). The only resume indicator.
  - Rank order: focused tab, other panes of its split, then background tabs (`ResumePool.resumeRank` ← `SidecarPool.resumeRank`).
  - Setting on: a quit (⌘Q, tray, last window on Windows) and "Restart to update" ask nothing (`quitNeedsConfirmation(risk, resumes)` in `quit-guard.ts`, `confirmQuit()` in `app-quit.ts`, the updater's `UPDATER_APPLY`); off, they ask before stopping running work.
- **One conversation stays one session across a relaunch**: a tab whose session file exists starts on it (`--session`), the continuation answers into that file, the sidebar lists it once. Pinned by `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (no second file, one indexed row, after kill and normal quit), `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (a)/(a2) and the SIGKILL test of `e2e/real-resume.e2e.ts`. Two rows once seen in the README resume clip were the fake omp writing a second journal (see the fake omp under Build, test), never OMPdesk.
- **The host owns the turns it runs for a paired device.**
  - On the device (A) a remote tab (`device`) is skipped entirely: no marker, no judging, no takeover by A's connectivity.
  - On the host (B) `index.ts` composes `TurnResume`'s pool from `SidecarPool` and `LocalHostServices` (`resumeFacts`/`startTab`/`setAwaitingConnection`/`noteStopped`/`tabForSession`, ids `peer:<deviceId>:<rtab>` kept when re-keyed; run bookkeeping via `trackRunEvent` in `sidecar-run-state.ts`; asks from the tab's `extensionUi` and the device's `extension_ui_response`; the device's Stop through `tab.command`). B writes the marker (with `peer`), continues after its own crash or quit (the marker revives the hosted tab, unstarted, waiting for its device in the grace period: `RemoteDevices.reviveHostTab`, only while hosting is on) and judges network failure by B's connectivity.
  - Grace (`HOST_TAB_GRACE_MS`, 10 min from link loss, `LocalHostServices.#startGrace`) closes only an idle hosted tab; one that runs out while the tab runs a turn (revived included) or holds it for B's connection starts another. A turn blocked on an ask waiting for the device keeps its omp until the device answers or B quits; no overall cap.
  - A only mirrors the wait (`awaitingConnection` tab event, emitted ahead of the failing events and re-sent on every `tab.open`; "Waiting for {device}'s internet connection…") and sends its Cancel to B (`RemoteSidecar.cancelHostWait` → `tab.cancelWait`).
  - A relaunched device reopens tabs under new rtabs: `tab.open` with a session path takes over the tab an earlier connection left (`#adopt`), so no second omp starts on the session.
  - One session file = one host tab = one omp. An open of a file this connection shows under another rtab, or another device's tab holds, is refused `session_open_in_tab`; `#open` re-checks the holder after its awaits; a tab whose omp moves onto a file another host tab holds (`#dedupe`, on its `session` identity) stops the other (keeping one that runs a turn) and tells its device `exited` (`host-services.test.ts`, three fail-first cases).
  - A host's session closes and deletes like a local one: its sidebar row (`SidebarDevices.tsx`) has Close (the tab; session stays on the host) and Delete (confirm names the host, `remote.sidebar.deleteMessage`; tab closes first, then `sessions.delete` on the host). `#deleteSession` releases the asking device's own idle tabs on that file, waits for their omp to exit, and refuses while one runs a turn or another device's live tab holds it (`host-services.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, the delete test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`: gone on the host and after both apps relaunch).
- **② Connectivity.** `ConnectivityMonitor` (net.isOnline() sampled by edge, windows' `online`/`offline` via `resume.reportOnline`, `powerMonitor` suspend/resume with a DNS probe until the wake is real, then `RESUME_WINDOW_MS` = 60 s) decides `shouldWait()`. A turn ending in a network-class error (`isNetworkError`, subset of stock's retryable pattern) while `shouldWait()` holds is not failed: the tab shows `awaitingConnection` (`TurnStatusRow`'s "Waiting for the internet connection…", Cancel → `resume.cancelWait`; `TurnErrorNotice` hidden), keeps a `network` marker and runs stock `retry` once online. A new run on the held tab (a prompt sent meanwhile, not the taken-over retry saga still ending) replaces the wait.
- **Tests**: `connectivity.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts` (marker transitions incl. crash, skip rules, rank order), `remote/host-services.test.ts` (hosted facts, revive, adoption, revived turn outliving the grace, open asks re-sent on re-attach), `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (SIGKILL and normal quit, Stop, setting off, offline wait/Cancel/online; en/ko × light/dark), `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` (host/device crash, host offline wait across a lost link and Cancel, a question across a cut link with and without host omp restart; two apps), `e2e/real-resume.e2e.ts` (opt-in, real omp). The fake omp implements `composer.restore_interrupted`, `fixture network error`, `/retry`.

### What a relaunch continues beyond the cut-off turn
Measured on the real omp by `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`; "Restart to update" = an approved quit while work runs).
- Stock: a foreground bash call is journaled "outcome unknown" and runs again; subagents (awaited or background) are revived by `restoreSession` (the parent gets the result, a waiting `wait` included, when stock continues its turn); an open ask/approval reopens.
- OMPdesk handles four things stock leaves behind:
  - **Background bash jobs** (`async: true`) started by the last process and never settled are named to the agent in one follow-up (`ompdesk-restart-jobs`; bridge `composer.restore_interrupted` → `resources/gui-bridge/restart-core.ts` `interruptedJobs`, reported as `jobs`). The model gets `restartFollowUp`'s text (each job `- <id>: <intent> — \`command\``, intent = the call's own `i` argument, then "start again only the jobs the work still needs, say nothing about the restart or this notice, carry on"). The transcript gets one muted line ("Re-running N background task(s) the restart stopped", `RestartNote`, `data-restart-jobs`, intents as hover title): `details` hold only job ids and intents, never a user bubble, never a raw command (a hidden `display: false` message left that to chance). Jobs are read from entries journaled before this process started, from after the second-to-last `session_exit`, never cut at a `session_exit` alone; a job an earlier relaunch already named counts as settled. Pinned on stock by the SIGKILL test of `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (no `session_exit` in its journal).
  - **Subagents revived for a session whose own turn did not go on**: same follow-up (next subsection).
  - **A goal active until the last process ended** (`goalActiveAtExit`: the last `mode_change` journaled before this process started, `performance.timeOrigin`, is `goal`; NEVER read it from a `session_exit`, which stock does not journal for SIGKILL/crash nor always when stdin closes mid-turn: omp 18.11.3 journaled only the aborted turn and an Autopilot run came back paused `app_closed`) that stock paused at startup comes back as `goalPausedByExit`. `TurnResume` resumes it once after the restore (`resumeGoal`, injectable; default local `set_goal` `resume`; `goalSettled` tells Autopilot `resumed`, `none` or `cancelled`).
  - **A new session's first turn cut off before stock wrote its file** (at the first assistant message): the pool keeps the prompt (`unsavedPrompt`, marker `prompt`) and the relaunch sends it again when restore and retry find nothing; it leaves the marker before sending, so once. The saved layout drops the missing path (`tab-layout.ts`), so the tab returns as a fresh session in the same folder and `TurnResume.launch` takes it for the marker (`#unwrittenSessionTab`: a local tab in the marker's folder with no session file, one per marker), never a hidden second one; only without such a tab does `openTab` open one. A spawn whose `--session` file does not exist starts fresh in its folder (`#spawn` in `sidecar.ts`, fresh-launch config so no older session auto-resumes; stock exits "Session not found" on it; also covers an omp restart of a never-used tab).
- Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`, `sidecar.test.ts`, `bridge-restart.test.ts` (intent, notice wording, details without a command), `MessageBubble.test.tsx` (muted line, not a bubble), `ChatStream.turn-status.test.tsx` (row's Stop), `quit-guard.test.ts`, `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (background-job relaunch: no toast, one resuming row, one muted line, no bubble, transcript at the bottom; idle session with a working background subagent: one muted subagents line and a turn; setting off: the quit asks, "Quit anyway" leaves no marker). Real omp (`(b2)`, 18 Oct 2026) records `ui` and `saidAfterNotice`: no restart narration from the model.

### Revived subagents
- **Seen**: the bridge's `composer.restore_interrupted` hands stock `restoreSession` the buses a live `task` run reports on (the task tool's `ToolSession` `eventBus`/`subagentEventBus`; TaskTool keeps it in a TS-private `session` field, `getToolByName("task")` in `capabilities.ts`), as `omp --restore` passes main.ts's own. Without them a revived subagent delivered its result but omp's RPC registry (`get_subagents`, the `subagent_*` frames the work board, dock and composer cue read) never heard of it (v0.35.1). Pinned by `src/main/bridge-restore-subagents-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, scripted loopback provider, no spend: a SIGKILL-cut background subagent is listed `running` after restore).
- **Told, when the session is idle**: if stock revived subagents but `turn` is false, the session would sit silent until an async result arrives (maybe hours). Causes (18 Oct 2026): the turn had ended and only background subagents kept the marker, or stock declined to continue (omp 18.12.1: parent in `wait`, session had used `eval`, so stock's `eval-state-context` message follows the interrupted-turn record and `restoreInterruptedTurn` and `/retry` answer "Nothing to retry"; oh-my-pi#16195, fixed in omp 18.12.2 whose `conversationEnd` looks past trailing eval-state-context messages; `MIN_OMP_VERSION` still admits older omp, where the follow-up is what wakes it).
  - The bridge adds them to the relaunch's one follow-up (`restartFollowUp(jobs, outcome.turn ? [] : revivedSubagents(branch, outcome.subagents))`; customType `ompdesk-restart-subagents`, or `ompdesk-restart-jobs` when jobs come too: one message, one turn). The model gets `- <id>: <intent>` (intent = the `task` assignment's `Title:` line, else its first line, `assignmentHeadline`, cut at 120 chars; a subagent's child has its id alone) and is told to check them, tell the user in a sentence or two what is in progress, keep supervising, and say nothing about the restart.
  - The transcript shows one muted line ("Continuing N background task(s) after the restart" / "재시작 뒤 백그라운드 작업 N개를 이어서 진행해요", `RestartNote`, `data-restart-subagents`, intents as hover title) from `details.subagents`. A restored turn gets none (its cut-off `wait` reruns and finds the revived job).
  - The bridge reports `followUp: true`; `TurnResume` counts revived subagents as continuing, so nothing is retried on top; a launch restores once per marker, so the follow-up comes once per relaunch.
  - Pinned by `bridge-restore-subagents-stock.test.ts` (an ended turn gets one follow-up; a cut-off `wait` in an eval journal is restored by stock ≥ 18.12.2 with none and gets one on older omp, the test reading the installed `omp --version`; a `wait` stock continues gets none), `bridge-restart.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx`, `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts`.

### Relaunch log
- Every relaunch decision goes to `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` (source `turn-resume`): `launch` {markers, enabled}; one `marker` per marker {session, reason, tab, via: layout | unwritten | opened | none, peer}; one `continue` per continuation {tab, action, restored: turn | subagents:N,jobs:N | nothing | failed | skipped, retried, prompt, goal, started, followUp, cancelled, restoreError/restoreCode, retryError/retryCode} or {ready: false} when its omp never came up.
- `retried: false` means no retry continued anything, not that none was sent: a failed restore always falls back to stock `retry`, both errors land on the line. A tab that "did not continue" is explained from the user's own files. Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`.

### Branch from here (fork)
- `sessions.fork_from` (message row's Branch, tree's "Open in new window"; rules in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/session-fork-core.ts` `forkCut`, used by the bridge and the fake omp) NEVER ends the copy on something nothing will answer: stock reads that as a cut-off turn (`findUnfinishedTurn`: the next omp journals "Previous OMP process exited before completing the turn." and a restore would run it).
  - From a user prompt the copy ends before it and the prompt returns as `draft` (stock `branch` semantics): `forkSessionFromEntryInNewTab` fills the new tab's composer (text and pictures); `fileBranchDraft` files the text as the new session's saved draft (`draft-persistence.ts`) for the tree's new window and a branch whose tab could not open.
  - Inside a tool batch the copy runs through the recorded results (stock `fork(entryId)` `#resolveForkLeaf`); a batch with a call still awaiting its result is cut before its assistant message.
  - On touch (`useShellInput() === "touch"`) Branch sits in the message row's ⋯ `MenuButton`, never a direct button a scroll-stop tap can hit (v0.35.1: a phone Branch under a just-sent prompt made a same-titled session that looked like a turn the restart never continued).
  - Pinned by `bridge-session-fork-stock.test.ts`, `messages.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx` (touch), `e2e/branch-from-prompt.e2e.ts`.
- **A fork shows where it came from**, so one keeping its parent's title never reads as a second copy (the user's two "추천 프롬프트 중복" rows): stock's `parentSession` header (`SessionInfo.parentSessionPath`, a path or, after a move, the parent's id) → `sessionForkOrigin` (`lib/session-fork.ts`) gives the parent's title, screen-reader words and one tooltip line "분기 · <parent> · <time>" / "Branch · <parent> · <time>" (`session.fork.*`).
